Codimension one intersections between components of the Emerton-Gee stack for GL 2 \GL_{2}
Kalyani Kansal

Abstract
. Let p p be a fixed odd prime, and let K K be a finite extension of 𝐐 p \mathbf{Q}_{p} with ring of integers 𝒪 K \mathcal{O}_{K} . The Emerton-Gee stack for GL 2 \GL_{2} is a stack of ( φ , Γ ) (\varphi,\Gamma) -modules. The stack, denoted 𝒳 2 \mathcal{X}_{2} , can be interpreted as a moduli stack of representations of the absolute Galois group of K K with p p -adic coefficients. The reduced part of the Emerton-Gee stack, denoted 𝒳 2 , red \mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}} , is an algebraic stack defined over a finite field of characteristic p p and can be viewed as a moduli stack of Galois representations with mod p p coefficients. The irreducible components of 𝒳 2 , red \mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}} are labelled in a natural way by Serre weights, which are the irreducible mod p p representations of GL 2 ⁡ ( 𝒪 K ) \GL_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K}) . Each irreducible component of 𝒳 2 , red \mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}} has dimension [ K : 𝐐 p ] [K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}] . In this article, we compute GL 2 ⁡ ( 𝒪 K ) \GL_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K}) -extensions of pairs of Serre weights and, motivated by the conjectural categorical p p -adic Langlands programme, we show that a non-trivial extension of a pair of non-isomorphic Serre weights implies a codimension 1 1 intersection of the corresponding irreducible components. The converse of this statement is true if the Serre weights are chosen to be sufficiently generic. Furthermore, we show that the number of top-dimensional components in a codimension 1 1 intersection is related to the depth of the extensions of the corresponding Serre weights.



MSC Classification Mathematics Subject Classification
11S99

1.
Introduction

Let
p
be a fixed, odd prime and let
K/\mathbf{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension, with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, residue field
k
and absolute Galois group
G_{K}
. In
[
EG2
]
, Emerton and Gee constructed and studied the stack of rank
d
étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules defined over the formal spectrum of a ring of integers
\mathcal{O}
in a finite extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
. The stack is denoted
\mathcal{X}_{d}
. Over Artinian coefficients, there exists an equivalence of categories between rank
d
étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules and
d
-dimensional
G_{K}
-representations that allows one to view
\mathcal{X}_{d}
as a moduli stack of Galois representations.

We now recall the following theorem by Emerton and Gee on the geometry of the reduced part of
\mathcal{X}_{d}
, which in the case
d=2
will provide the setting for this article.

Theorem 1.1
.

[
EG2
, Thm. 1.2.1]

The reduced part of
\mathcal{X}_{d}
, denoted by
\mathcal{X}_{d,\operatorname{red}}
, is an algebraic stack of finite type over
\mathbf{F}_{p}
. The irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{d,\operatorname{red}}
admit a natural labeling by Serre weights. Each irreducible component of
\mathcal{X}_{d,\operatorname{red}}
has dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]d(d-1)/2
.

Here, the Serre weights are the irreducible mod
p
representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{d}(k)
, and by inflation of
\mathrm{GL}_{d}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
.
The present work is an attempt towards relating the representation theory of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
in terms of extensions of Serre weights with the structure of the intersections of the irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}
. Our investigation is motivated by a conjectural categorical
p
-adic Langlands correspondence, as we explain below.

1.1.
Categorical
p
-adic Langlands

The Emerton-Gee stack
\mathcal{X}_{d}
is expected to play a central role in the
p
-adic Langlands program, occupying the position played by the moduli stack of
L
-parameters in the work of Fargues-Scholze on the classical Langlands correspondence. More precisely,
[
EGH
, Conj. 6.1.6]
proposes the existence of an exact and fully faithful functor

\mathfrak{A}:D^{b}_{f.p.}(\text{sm.}\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K))\to D^{b}_{\text{coh}}(\mathcal{X}_{d}),

satisfying a number of properties related to duality and support, and witnessing the
p
-adic Langlands correspondence. The domain
D^{b}_{f.p.}(\text{sm.}\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K))
is a derived category of smooth representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{d}(K)
while the codomain
D^{b}_{\text{coh}}(\mathcal{X}_{d})
is a derived category of quasicoherent sheaves on
\mathcal{X}_{d}
. We omit the details of how these categories are precisely defined and refer the interested reader to
[
EGH
, Ch. 6]
instead.

Specializing to
d=2
, the conditions relating to duality and support that the functor
\mathfrak{A}
is expected to satisfy imply the following: If
\sigma
is a Serre weight that is non-Steinberg (a notion that will be defined in
Section

1.5
), the support of
\mathfrak{A}(\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}\sigma)
is the irreducible component of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}
labeled by
\sigma
, which we denote by
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}
. This follows from
[
EGH
, Conj. 6.1.34]
and
[
CEGS
, Thm. 1.2]
.
Now, let
\sigma
and
\tau
be two non-isomorphic non-Steinberg Serre weights, and let

0\to\sigma\to V\to\tau\to 0

be a short exact sequence of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
-modules inducing the short exact sequence

0\to\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}\sigma\to\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}V\to\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}\tau\to 0

of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)
-modules. Since
\mathfrak{A}
is (conjecturally) an exact and fully faithful functor, the extension class of
\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}V
will be witnessed precisely on the intersection of support of
\mathfrak{A}\circ\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}\sigma
and of
\mathfrak{A}\circ\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}\tau
, that is on
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
.

Thus, away from
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
, the sheaf coming from
V
will be isomorphic to the direct sum of sheaves coming from
\sigma
and
\tau
. In particular, if
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
is empty, we will have the following diagram of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
-representations where the right downward arrow splits:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{c-Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(K)}V}

The horizontal arrows split as maps of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
-representations by Mackey’s decomposition theorem. The left vertical arrow must then split as well, and
V
must be isomorphic to
\sigma\oplus\tau
. Thus, if the conjectured functor
\mathfrak{A}
exists, then

\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}=\varnothing\implies\Ext^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})]}(\sigma,\tau)=0.

This analysis only depends on the conjectured support of the sheaves coming from
\sigma
and
\tau
, and no other properties. As we will see, finer structural details of the intersections of components could be correlated to extensions of the corresponding Serre weights.

1.2.
Main results

In this article, we specifically study codimension
1
intersections of irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}
in conjunction with extensions of Serre weights. Our first main result is the following (see
Theorem

7.1
):

Theorem 1.2
.

If
\sigma
and
\tau
are a pair of non-isomorphic Serre weights, then

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})]}(\sigma,\tau)\neq 0\implies\mathrm{dim}\;\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}=[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1.

The converse implication is not always true. However, when both
\sigma
and
\tau
are weakly regular, the converse is also true.

The notion of weak regularity mentioned in the theorem above is a certain genericity condition on the Serre weights and is defined precisely in
Section

1.5
. In the course of proving
Theorem

1.2
, we obtain the following result on extensions of Serre weights that may be of independent interest in the representation theory of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
(see
Theorem

2.20
and
Theorem

2.26
).

Theorem 1.3
.

If
K/\mathbf{Q}_{p}
is unramified and
\sigma
and
\tau
are a pair of non-isomorphic weakly regular Serre weights, then the natural map

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL_{2}}(k)]}(\sigma,\tau)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL_{2}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})]}(\sigma,\tau)

is an isomorphism.

If
K/\mathbf{Q}_{p}
is ramified, then for any pair of non-isomorphic Serre weights
\sigma
and
\tau
, there exists an exact sequence

0\to\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL_{2}}(k)]}(\sigma,\tau)\to\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL_{2}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})]}(\sigma,\tau)\to\Hom_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma,H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\tau))\to 0

where
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
is the kernel of the natural quotient map
\GL_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})\to\GL_{2}(k)
.

The results of
Section

2
show that when
p\neq 5
and
\sigma
and
\tau
are non-isomorphic Serre weights with the space
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})]}(\sigma,\tau)
non-zero, then
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})]}(\sigma,\tau)
is
1
-dimensional. A generator of this space can either arise as a
\GL_{2}(k)
-extension or at a deeper level as an element of
\Hom_{\GL_{2}(k)}(\sigma,H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\tau))
. The depth at which the extensions arise curiously relates to the number of top dimensional components in
{\mathcal{X}}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
. By
Theorem

1.3
above, generically, the differences in depth are relevant only in the ramified setting. This and other differences in the structure of intersections in the unramified and ramified settings are summarized below (see
Theorem

7.3
).

Theorem 1.4
.

Let
\sigma
and
\tau
be two weakly regular, non-isomorphic Serre weights such that
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
is of codimension
1
. Then the following are true:

(1)

When
K
is unramified over
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, the number of components of dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
in
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
is
1
. When
K
is ramified over
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, this number is
2
if the
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(k)
-extensions of
\tau
by
\sigma
are non-trivial, and
1
otherwise.

(2)

When
K
is unramified over
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, a component of dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
in
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
is not contained in
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma^{\prime}}
for any non-Steinberg
\sigma^{\prime}
not isomorphic to
\sigma
or
\tau
. In the ramified case, for sufficiently generic
\sigma
and
\tau
(c.f.
Theorem

7.3
), a component of dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
in
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
is contained in

{\mathcal{X}}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma^{\prime}}
for some non-Steinberg
\sigma^{\prime}
not isomorphic to
\sigma
or
\tau
.

We emphasize that
Theorem

1.4
requires the “weakly regular” hypothesis, and one can find counterexamples to each of the statements above in the non-generic case.

1.3.
Strategy

Our strategy involves two separate investigations: the first determines the
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
-extensions of Serre weights yielding
Theorem

1.3
and the second determines by hand the pairs of Serre weights for which the corresponding irreducible components intersect in codimension
1
. In the end, we compare the results of the two investigations obtaining
Theorem

1.2
. A close examination of the representations that contribute to a codimension
1
intersection of irreducible components allows us to further conclude
Theorem

1.4
. We now explain approximately the ideas that go into the two investigations.

In order to compute
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
-extensions of a pair of Serre weights
\sigma
and
\tau
, we utilize a Grothendieck spectral sequence to construct a left exact sequence

0\to\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL_{2}}(k)]}(\sigma,\tau)\to\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}]}(\sigma,\tau)\to\Hom_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma,H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\tau)).

Explicit descriptions of the two terms flanking the group of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
-extensions are available in
[
BP
]
. The difficulty is in computing the image of the map

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\mathrm{GL_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}]}(\sigma,\tau)\to\Hom_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma,H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\tau)).

We resolve this (in most cases) by writing down cocycles of
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
and attempting to extend them by hand to
\GL_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
.

The starting point of the second investigation comes from the following key theorem
[
CEGS
, Thm. 1.2]
by Caraiani, Emerton, Gee and Savitt describing the finite type points of the irreducible components of
{\mathcal{X}}_{2,\operatorname{red}}
. Recall that to each
\overline{\rho}\colon G_{K}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{p})
one can associate a set
W(\overline{\rho})
of Serre weights (see
Section

1.5
for details).

Theorem 1.5
.

[
CEGS
, Thm. 1.2]

Let
\sigma
be a non-Steinberg Serre weight. The
\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{p}
-points of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}
are precisely the representations
\overline{\rho}\colon G_{K}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{p})
satisfying
\sigma\in W(\overline{\rho})
.

Fixing non-Steinberg non-isomorphic Serre weights
\sigma
and
\tau
, we proceed by looking for families of representations that have both
\sigma
and
\tau
as Serre weights, and therefore give points of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
. The sizes of these families can then be used to determine the dimension of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
. As employed in
[
EG2
]
, a source of families of representations is provided by extensions of fixed
G_{K}
-characters together with extensions of their unramified twists. Every irreducible component of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}
can be obtained as the closure of such a family. Vector spaces of extensions of fixed
G_{K}
-characters are typically
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]
-dimensional. Allowing various unramified twists of the fixed characters adds
2
to the dimension, while one dimension is taken away because a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-orbit of an extension class gives the same representation and yet another dimension is taken away because of a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-worth of endomorphisms of each extension. Thus a codimension
1
intersection of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
may be expected to correspond to the existence of a codimension
1
family of extensions of fixed
G_{K}
-characters (as well as their unramified twists) with both
\sigma
and
\tau
as their Serre weights. We use detailed descriptions in
[
DDR
,
Ste
]
of Serre weights associated to extensions of
G_{K}
-characters to explicitly compute such families.

1.4.
Outline of the paper

In
Section

2
, we compute the group of extensions of Serre weights as
\GL_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
-representations. In
Section

3
, we relate the dimensions of families of
G_{K}
-representations with both
\sigma
and
\tau
as Serre weights to the dimension of
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
. We also relate the number of sufficiently large families to the number of components of maximal dimension inside
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
. The objective of
Section

4
is to recall explicit criteria for computations of Serre weights of representations as described in
[
DDR
,
Ste
]
. Along with the results of
Section

3
, these criteria are used to relate the existence of a codimension
1
intersection between
{\mathcal{X}}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\sigma}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{2,\operatorname{red}}^{\tau}
to a requirement that
\sigma
and
\tau
satisfy a precise numerical relationship. This numerical relationship is seen to manifest in two distinct types: type I and type II, defined in
Section

4.3
. In sections
Sections

5
and
6
, we compute all the pairs
\sigma
and
\tau
that satisfy the aforementioned numerical relationship of the two types. Finally,
Section

7
collects all the findings.

1.5.
Notation

Let
p>2
be a fixed prime and let
K
be a finite extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, residue field
k
and uniformizer
\pi
. The requirement on
p
is to allow the key input of
[
CEGS
, Cor. 7.2]
.

We let
f:=f(K/\mathbf{Q}_{p})
and
e:=e(K/\mathbf{Q}_{p})
. Let
G_{K}
be the absolute Galois group of
K
, and
I_{K}
the inertia group. Let
E
be a finite extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
in a fixed algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
so that all embeddings of
K
into
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
are contained in
E
. Let
{\mathcal{O}}
be the ring of integers of
E
with residue field
\mathbf{F}
. Denote by
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
the residue field of the maximal unramified extension of
E
inside
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We will consider representations of the groups
\GL_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
,
\GL_{2}(k)
,
G_{K}
and
I_{K}
in various settings with coefficients in algebraic extensions of
\mathbf{F}
. Equipping algebraic extensions of finite fields with discrete topology, all representations will be continuous.

Fix an embedding
\theta_{f-1}:k\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{F}}
. Let
\theta_{f-1-i}:=\theta_{f-1}^{p^{i}}
for
i\in\mathbf{Z}
. Since
\theta_{i}=\theta_{i+f}
, we will view the indices
i
of the embeddings as elements of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
. Let
\omega_{i}
be the
I_{K}
-character given by

\omega_{i}(g)=\theta_{i}(\frac{g(\sqrt[p^{f}-1]{\pi})}{\sqrt[p^{f}-1]{\pi}}).

Let
\mathbf{t}=(t_{i})_{i}
and
\mathbf{s}=(s_{i})_{i}
be tuples of length
f
indexed over elements of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
. We let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
denote the irreducible
\GL_{2}(k)
-representation

\bigotimes_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}(\mathrm{det}^{t_{i}}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{s_{i}}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}

where
k^{2}
is the standard two-dimensional representation of
\GL_{2}(k)
and each
s_{i}\in[0,p-1]
. All irreducible
\GL_{2}(k)
-representations with coefficients in
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
are of this form and are called Serre weights. We can uniquely identify each Serre weight by
\mathbf{s}
and
\mathbf{t}
if we demand that
t_{i}\in[0,p-1]\;\forall i
and at least one of the
t_{i}
’s is not
p-1
. Following
[
Gee
]
, we say
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
is
weakly regular
, if each
s_{i}\in[0,p-2]
. We say that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
is
Steinberg
if each
s_{i}
equals
p-1
; we say it is
non-Steinberg
otherwise.

Normalize Hodge-Tate weights in such a way that all Hodge-Tate weights of the cyclotomic character are equal to
-1
. Consistent with the conventions in
[
EG2
]
,
we say that a representation
\overline{\rho}\colon G_{K}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
has Serre weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
if
\overline{\rho}
has a crystalline lift
\rho\colon G_{K}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
that satisfies the following condition: For each embedding
\theta_{i}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbf{F}
, there is an embedding
\tilde{\sigma}_{i}:K\hookrightarrow E
lifting
\theta_{i}
such that the
\tilde{\sigma}_{i}
labeled Hodge-Tate weights of
\rho
are
\{t_{i},s_{i}+t_{i}+1\}
, and the remaining
(e-1)f
pairs of Hodge-Tate weights of
\rho
are all
\{0,1\}
. In this situation, we say
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}\in W(\overline{\rho})
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{2,\operatorname{red}}
, or simply
\mathcal{X}
, be the reduced part of the Emerton-Gee stack for two-dimensional representations of
G_{K}
. It is defined over
\mathbf{F}
and is an algebraic stack of pure dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]
. The irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}
are indexed by the non-Steinberg Serre weights. For a non-Steinberg Serre weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
, we denote the corresponding irreducible component by
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}
.

1.6.
Acknowledgements

This work was done as part of my PhD dissertation. I thank my advisor David Savitt for sharing the problem with me along with his insights on it, as well as providing continual guidance and support. 2.
Extensions of Serre weights

Denote by
{\mathcal{K}}
the group
\GL_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
and by
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}
the subgroup
1+\pi^{n}M_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
for
n\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}
. For all representations and cohomology groups in this section, the field of coefficients will be
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
and all homomorphisms we consider will at least be
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-homomorphisms. We will suppress the field of coefficients from the notation. Additionally, we will view all
\GL_{2}(k)
-representations as
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}
- and
{\mathcal{K}}
-representations by inflation. Our objective in this section is to compute the group of
{\mathcal{K}}
-extensions of Serre weights.

It is well-known that if
\sigma
and
\tau
are
\GL_{2}(k)
-representations, then there exists an isomorphism

(2.1)

\displaystyle\Ext^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}}(\sigma,\tau)\cong H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n},\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau).

Indeed, in the category of
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}
-representations, one can take a projective resolution of
\sigma
by free
\overline{\mathbf{F}}[{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}]
-modules. As a
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}
-representation,

\Hom(\overline{\mathbf{F}}[{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}],\tau)\cong\Ind_{1}^{{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}}\tau.

The isomorphism of
Equation

2.1
follows from the observation that induced representations constitute a class of adapted objects (in the sense of homological algebra) for the functor that sends a
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}
-representation to the vector space of
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}
-invariants. This is because of Shapiro’s lemma and the fact that if
V
is a
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}
-representation, then
V
is a subobject of
\Ind_{1}^{{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}}\mathrm{res}_{1}^{{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}}V
. Taking colimits, we obtain

(2.2)

\displaystyle\Ext^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma,\tau)\cong H^{1}({\mathcal{K}},\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau).

Henceforth, we will freely use the identifications in
Equations

2.1
and
2.2
. Next, for each
n
, we consider the left exact sequence of low degree terms obtained from the inflation-restriction spectral sequence for
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}
. Passing to colimits as
n
varies, we obtain

(2.3)

\displaystyle 0\to H^{1}(\GL_{2}(k),\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathit{inf}}H^{1}({\mathcal{K}},\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathit{res}}H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\sigma)^{\GL_{2}(k)}

where
\mathit{inf}
is the map obtained by pre-composing a
\GL_{2}(k)
-cocycle with the natural quotient
{\mathcal{K}}\to\GL_{2}(k)
and the map
\mathit{res}
is obtained by restricting a continuous
{\mathcal{K}}
-cocycle to
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
. The following proposition describes the group
H^{1}(\GL_{2}(k),\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau)
when
\sigma
and
\tau
are Serre weights.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be a pair of Serre weights. Then,

\dim H^{1}(\GL_{2}(k),\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})\leq 1.

The dimension is
1
if and only if one of the following holds:

(1)

f=1
and either

(a)

s_{0}<p-2
,
s^{\prime}_{0}=p-s_{0}-3
and
t^{\prime}_{0}\equiv t_{0}+s_{0}+1\mod p-1
; or

(b)

s_{0}\not\in\{0,p-1\}
,
s^{\prime}_{0}=p-s_{0}-1
and
t^{\prime}_{0}\equiv t_{0}+s_{0}\mod p-1
.

(2)

f>1
and either

(a)

\exists j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
such that

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}s_{i}&\text{ for }i\not\in\{j-1,j\},\\
s_{i}-1&\text{ for }i=j-1,\\
p-s_{i}-2&\text{ for }i=j.\end{cases}

and

\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}t^{\prime}_{i}p^{f-1-i}\equiv\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}t_{i}p^{f-1-i}+(s_{j}+1)p^{f-1-j}\mod p^{f}-1;

or

(b)

\exists j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
such that

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}s_{i}&\text{ for }i\not\in\{j-1,j\},\\
s_{i}+1&\text{ for }i=j-1,\\
p-s_{i}-2&\text{ for }i=j.\end{cases}

and

\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}t^{\prime}_{i}p^{f-1-i}\equiv\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}t_{i}p^{f-1-i}-(p-s_{j}-1)p^{f-1-j}\mod p^{f}-1.

Proof.

This is
[
BP
, Cor. 4.6]
. ∎

In the subsequent text, we will use explicit bases for Serre weights which we now describe.
Recall that
k^{2}
denotes the standard two-dimensional representation of
\GL_{2}(k)
.
If
\{x,y\}
is a basis of
k^{2}
, then a basis of
\Sym^{s_{i}}k^{2}
is given by homogeneous monomials in
x
and
y
of degree
s_{i}
, namely
\{x^{k_{i}}z^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\}_{k_{i}}
where the indexing set is the set of integers
k_{i}\in[0,s_{i}]
. This induces a basis of
(\mathrm{det}^{t_{i}}\otimes\Sym^{s_{i}}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}
given by
\{(1\otimes x^{k_{i}}y^{s_{i}-k_{i}})\otimes 1\}_{k_{i}}
, which we will write simply as
\{x^{k_{i}}y^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\}_{k_{i}}
. We will call
\{x^{k_{i}}y^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\}_{k_{i}}
the basis of
(\mathrm{det}^{t_{i}}\otimes\Sym^{s_{i}}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}
induced from
\{x,y\}
. Putting these bases together for each
i
, we obtain a basis of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
given by

\{\otimes_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}x^{k_{i}}y^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathfrak{K}}

where the indexing set
\mathfrak{K}
for the basis is the set of tuples
\mathbf{k}=(k_{i})_{i}
with
k_{i}\in[0,s_{i}]
for each
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
. We will call this the basis of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}

induced from
\{x,y\}
.

Proposition 2.5
.

Let
\sigma
be a
\GL_{2}(k)
-representation. There exists an isomorphism of
\GL_{2}(k)
-representations

(2.6)

\displaystyle H^{1}({{\mathcal{K}}}_{1},\sigma)\cong\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\bigl(\sigma\otimes\big((\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}\bigr)\bigr)\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\sigma

where
d
is the dimension of
\Hom(1+\pi\mathcal{O}_{K},\overline{\mathbf{F}})
, such that the following hold true:

(1)

Suppose
\{u,v\}
is a basis of
k^{2}
and
\{u^{2},uv,v^{2}\}
the induced basis of
(\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}
. Then, the inclusion

\sigma\otimes\big((\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}\bigr)\>\hookrightarrow\>H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\sigma)\cong\Hom({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\sigma)

induced by the isomorphism in
Equation

2.6
is given by

\displaystyle\alpha\otimes u^{2}\>

\displaystyle\mapsto\>\kappa_{i}^{l}\alpha

\displaystyle\alpha\otimes 2uv\>

\displaystyle\mapsto\>\epsilon_{i}\alpha

(2.7)

\displaystyle\alpha\otimes v^{2}\>

\displaystyle\mapsto\>\kappa_{i}^{u}\alpha

for any
\alpha\in\sigma
, where
\kappa_{i}^{l},\epsilon_{i},\kappa_{i}^{u}
are group homomorphisms
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}
given by

\displaystyle\kappa_{i}^{l}\biggl(\!\begin{pmatrix}1+\pi a&\pi b\\
\pi c&1+\pi d\end{pmatrix}\!\biggr)=\theta_{i}(c)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i}\biggl(\!\begin{pmatrix}1+\pi a&\pi b\\
\pi c&1+\pi d\end{pmatrix}\!\biggr)=\theta_{i}(a-d)

\displaystyle\kappa_{i}^{u}\biggl(\!\begin{pmatrix}1+\pi a&\pi b\\
\pi c&1+\pi d\end{pmatrix}\!\biggr)=\theta_{i}(b)

(2)

The inclusion
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\sigma\hookrightarrow H^{1}({{\mathcal{K}}}_{1},\sigma)
induced by
Equation

2.6
corresponds to group homomorphisms
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\to\sigma
that factor through the determinant and are not given by any of the cocyles appearing in
\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\bigl(\sigma\otimes\big((\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}\bigr)\bigr)
.

Proof.

By
[
BP
, Prop. 5.1]
.
∎

Corollary 2.8
.

If
\sigma
and
\tau
are
\GL_{2}(k)
-representations, then there exists an isomorphism

\Hom(\sigma,H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\tau))\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau).

Proof.

Via the isomorphism in
Equation

2.6
, one notes that
\sigma^{\vee}\otimes H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\tau)\cong H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau).

∎

Corollary 2.9
.

If
\sigma
and
\tau
are non-isomorphic Serre weights, then

\Ext^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma,\tau)\cong\Ext^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}(\sigma,\tau).

Proof.

We need to verify that every continuous cocycle from
{\mathcal{K}}
to
\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau
factors through
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}
. It suffices to check this after restricting the cocycle to
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
. We see from
Proposition

2.5
that since
\sigma
and
\tau
are non-isomorphic,

(2.10)

\displaystyle H^{1}({\mathcal{K}},\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau)^{\GL_{2}(k)}\cong\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\bigl(\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau\otimes\big((\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}\bigr)\bigr)^{\GL_{2}(k)}.

As described in
Proposition

2.5
(i), cocycles in
\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\tau\otimes\big((\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}\bigr)
are obtained by taking tensors with
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-valued cocycles
\kappa_{i}^{l}
,
\epsilon_{i}
and
\kappa_{i}^{u}
. Each of these three factors through
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}
, finishing the proof.
∎

Lemma 2.11
.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
be a Serre weight. Suppose
\{x,y\}
is the standard basis of
k^{2}
inducing the basis
\{\otimes_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}x^{k_{i}}y^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\}_{\mathbf{k}}
of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
. Use the same notation to denote the induced basis of
\sigma_{\mathbf{-t-s},\mathbf{s}}
.
Then
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\cong\sigma_{\mathbf{-t-s},\mathbf{s}}
under the following map:

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathbf{-t-s},\mathbf{s}}

\displaystyle\otimes_{i}(x^{k_{i}}y^{s_{i}-k_{i}})^{\vee}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\otimes_{i}\binom{s_{i}}{k_{i}}x^{s_{i}-k_{i}}(-y)^{k_{i}}

Proof.

By direct computation.
∎

Corollary 2.12
.

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}^{\vee})\neq 0
if and only if
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})\neq 0
.

Proof.

The key observation is that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\cong\sigma_{-\mathbf{t}-\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}}
is a twist of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
by a power of the determinant character. Thus, the statement of the corollary will hold as long as taking duals of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
involves twisting by the same character, or in other words, if
\forall a\in k^{\times}
,

\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\theta_{i}(a)^{2t_{i}+s_{i}}=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\theta_{i}(a)^{2t^{\prime}_{i}+s^{\prime}_{i}}.

Note that the left and right hand sides above give the central characters of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
respectively. The equality of the two is automatic if
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\GL_{2}(k)}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})\neq 0
because the group algebra of the center of
\GL_{2}(k)
is semisimple. Otherwise, using
Proposition

2.5
and
Corollary

2.8
, we note that if
H^{1}({\mathcal{K}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})^{\GL_{2}(k)}
is nonzero, then
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
is either isomorphic to
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
or is a subrepresentation of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}\otimes\big((\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}\bigr)
for some
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
. In either case, the equality of the central characters follows from the fact that
\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2}
has trivial central character.
∎

Corollary 2.13
.

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})\neq 0
if and only if
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}})\neq 0
.

Proof.

By
Corollary

2.12
, we have

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})\neq 0\iff\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}^{\vee})\neq 0.

Dualizing, the right hand side is equivalent to
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}})\neq 0
.∎

Proposition 2.14
.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
be a pair of non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights. Then
\dim H^{1}({\mathcal{K}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})^{\GL_{2}(k)}\leq 1
. The dimension is
1
if and only if there exists an
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
such that

\displaystyle s_{j}=\begin{cases}s^{\prime}_{j}\pm 2&\text{ if }j=i,\\
s^{\prime}_{j}&\text{ if }j\neq i,\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-j}t_{j}\equiv\mp p^{f-1-i}+\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-j}t^{\prime}_{j}\mod p^{f}-1.

Proof.

Proposition 5.4 and Corollary 5.5 in
[
BP
]
.∎

Corollary 2.15
.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
be a pair of non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights. Then
\dim H^{1}({\mathcal{K}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})^{\GL_{2}(k)}=\dim H^{1}({\mathcal{K}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}})^{\GL_{2}(k)}
.

Proof.

The three criteria listed in
Proposition

2.14
are satisfied if and only if they are satisfied upon interchanging
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
.∎

Our next step is to write down
\GL_{2}(k)
-invariant cocycles in
H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}})
as functions
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\to\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
. This will allow for an explicit description of the map
\mathit{res}
when
K
is unramified over
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, as we will see later. For now, the following lemma will be useful.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
r\leq p-3
. The following are true:

(1)

We have an isomorphism of
\GL_{2}(k)
-representations:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}^{2}k^{2}\otimes_{k}\mathrm{Sym}^{r}k^{2}\cong\mathrm{Sym}^{r+2}k^{2}\oplus(\Sym^{r}k^{2}\otimes\mathrm{det})\oplus(\Sym^{r-2}k^{2}\otimes\mathrm{det}^{2}).

(2)

Let bases of
\mathrm{Sym}^{r+2}k^{2}
,
\mathrm{Sym}^{2}k^{2}
and
\mathrm{Sym}^{r}k^{2}
be given by

\displaystyle\{w^{k}z^{r+2-k}\}_{k=0}^{r+2}

of

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}^{r+2}k^{2},

\displaystyle\{u^{j}v^{2-j}\}_{j=0}^{2}

of

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}^{2}k^{2},

and

\displaystyle\{x^{k}y^{r-k}\}_{k=0}^{r}

of

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}^{r}k^{2},

induced from the standard basis of
k^{2}
denoted
\{w,z\}
,
\{u,v\}
and
\{x,y\}
respectively. The inclusion

\Sym^{r+2}k^{2}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sym}^{2}k^{2}\otimes_{k}\mathrm{Sym}^{r}k^{2}

is given (uniquely upto scalar multiplication) as follows:

\displaystyle\quad w^{k}z^{r+2-k}\quad

\displaystyle\mapsto\qquad

\displaystyle u^{2}\otimes\dfrac{k(k-1)}{(r+2)(r+1)}x^{k-2}y^{r+2-k}

\displaystyle+2uv\otimes\dfrac{k(r+2-k)}{(r+2)(r+1)}x^{k-1}y^{r+1-k}

\displaystyle+v^{2}\otimes\dfrac{(r+2-k)(r+1-k)}{(r+2)(r+1)}x^{k}y^{r-k}

for
k\in[2,r]

\displaystyle\quad wz^{r+1}\quad

\displaystyle\mapsto\qquad

\displaystyle 2uv\otimes\dfrac{1}{r+2}y^{r}+v^{2}\otimes\dfrac{r}{r+2}xy^{r-1}

if
r>0

\displaystyle\quad w^{r+1}z\quad

\displaystyle\mapsto\qquad

\displaystyle u^{2}\otimes\dfrac{r}{r+2}x^{r-1}y+2uv\otimes\dfrac{1}{r+2}x^{r}

if
r>0

\displaystyle\quad wz\quad

\displaystyle\mapsto\qquad

\displaystyle uv\otimes 1

if
r=0

\displaystyle\quad w^{r+2}\quad

\displaystyle\mapsto\qquad

\displaystyle u^{2}\otimes x^{r}

\displaystyle\quad z^{r+2}\quad

\displaystyle\mapsto\qquad

\displaystyle v^{2}\otimes y^{r}

Proof.

The first statement is from
[
BP
, Prop. 5.4]
. The second statement is verified by direct comparison of
\GL_{2}(k)
-action.
∎

For the following proposition and theorem, we let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
be a pair of non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights such that for some
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
,

\displaystyle s_{j}=\begin{cases}s^{\prime}_{j}+2&\text{ if }j=i,\\
s^{\prime}_{j}&\text{ if }j\neq i,\end{cases}

and

(2.17)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-j}t_{j}\equiv-p^{f-1-i}+\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-j}t^{\prime}_{j}\mod p^{f}-1.

(These are the conditions from
Proposition

2.14
.) Further, let the bases of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be denoted by

\displaystyle\{\otimes_{j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}w^{k_{j}}z^{s_{j}-k_{j}}\}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathfrak{K}}

for

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}\text{ as well as for }\sigma_{\mathbf{-t-s},\mathbf{s}}\cong\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee},

and

\displaystyle\{\otimes_{j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}x^{k^{\prime}_{j}}y^{s^{\prime}_{j}-k^{\prime}_{j}}\}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}}\in\mathfrak{K^{\prime}}}

for

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}},

which are the bases induced from the standard basis of
k^{2}
denoted
\{w,z\}
in the case of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{-t-s},\mathbf{s}}
and denoted
\{x,y\}
in the case of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
. To be clear, for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}
, we are choosing the induced basis of
\sigma_{\mathbf{-t-s},\mathbf{s}}
as explained in the paragraph preceding
Proposition

2.5
, instead of the basis obtained by taking duals of the basis of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
. The relationship between our chosen basis and the dual basis is given in
Lemma

2.11
. Recall also that the set
\mathfrak{K}
(resp.
\mathfrak{K^{\prime}}
) is the set of all
f
-tuples
\mathbf{k}=(k_{j})_{j}
(resp.
\mathbf{k^{\prime}}=(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j}
) indexed over
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
such that for each
j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
,
k_{j}\in[0,s_{j}]
(resp.
k^{\prime}_{j}\in[0,s^{\prime}_{j}]
).

From these, we construct the obvious tensor product basis of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and for later convenience, we write the basis elements by positioning the
i
-th terms of the basis elements of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
at the end. Thus, a basis of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
is given by the set

(2.18)

\displaystyle\{\big((\otimes_{j\neq i}w^{k_{j}}z^{s_{j}-k_{j}})\otimes(\otimes_{j\neq i}x^{k^{\prime}_{j}}y^{s_{j}-k^{\prime}_{j}})\big){\otimes}\big(w^{k_{i}}z^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\otimes x^{k^{\prime}_{i}}y^{s_{i}-2-k^{\prime}_{i}}\big)\}_{(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k^{\prime}})}

indexed over the elements of
\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{K^{\prime}}
.

Proposition 2.19
.

For Serre weights
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
as above, the space of
\GL_{2}(k)
-invariant cocyles of
H^{1}({{\mathcal{K}}}_{1}/{{\mathcal{K}}}_{2},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})\cong H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2},\sigma_{-\mathbf{t}-\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})
is spanned by

\displaystyle\kappa_{i}^{l}A+\epsilon_{i}B+\kappa_{i}^{u}C

where
\kappa_{i}^{l}
,
\epsilon_{i}
and
\kappa_{i}^{u}
are homomorphisms
{{\mathcal{K}}}_{1}/{{\mathcal{K}}}_{2}\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}
defined in
Proposition

2.5
and
A
,
B
and
C
are elements of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
defined below.

If
s_{i}>2
,

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\sum\limits_{(k_{j})_{j\neq i}}(\otimes_{j\neq i}\binom{s_{j}}{k_{j}}w^{k_{j}}(-z)^{s_{j}-k_{j}})\otimes(\otimes_{j\neq i}x^{s_{j}-k_{j}}y^{k_{j}})\right){\otimes}

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \sum\limits_{k_{i}=2}^{s_{i}-2}\left(\binom{s_{i}}{k_{i}}w^{k_{i}}(-z)^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\otimes\dfrac{(s_{i}-k_{i})(s_{i}-k_{i}-1)}{s_{i}(s_{i}-1)}x^{s_{i}-k_{i}-2}y^{k_{i}}\right)\\
+\left(w(-z)^{s_{i}-1}\otimes(s_{i}-2)x^{s_{i}-3}y\right)\\
+\left((-z)^{s_{i}}\otimes x^{s_{i}-2}\right)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{(k_{j})_{j\neq i}}(\otimes_{j\neq i}\binom{s_{j}}{k_{j}}w^{k_{j}}(-z)^{s_{j}-k_{j}})\otimes(\otimes_{j\neq i}x^{s_{j}-k_{j}}y^{k_{j}})\right){\otimes}

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \sum\limits_{k_{i}=2}^{s_{i}-2}\left(\binom{s_{i}}{k_{i}}w^{k_{i}}(-z)^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\otimes\dfrac{(s_{i}-k_{i})k_{i}}{s_{i}(s_{i}-1)}x^{s_{i}-k_{i}-1}y^{k_{i}-1}\right)\\
+\left(w^{s_{i}-1}(-z)\otimes y^{s_{i}-2}\right)\\
+\left(w(-z)^{s_{i}-1}\otimes x^{s_{i}-2}\right)\\
\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{(k_{j})_{j\neq i}}(\otimes_{j\neq i}\binom{s_{j}}{k_{j}}w^{k_{j}}(-z)^{s_{j}-k_{j}})\otimes(\otimes_{j\neq i}x^{s_{j}-k_{j}}y^{k_{j}})\right){\otimes}

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \sum\limits_{k_{i}=2}^{s_{i}-2}\left(\binom{s_{i}}{k_{i}}w^{k_{i}}(-z)^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\otimes\dfrac{k_{i}(k_{i}-1)}{s_{i}(s_{i}-1)}x^{s_{i}-k_{i}}y^{k_{i}-2}\right)\\
+\left(w^{s_{i}-1}(-z)\otimes(s_{i}-2)xy^{s_{i}-3}\right)\\
+\left(w^{s_{i}}\otimes y^{s_{i}-2}\right)\end{aligned}\right)

If
s_{i}=2
,

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{(k_{j})_{j\neq i}}(\otimes_{j\neq i}\binom{s_{j}}{k_{j}}w^{k_{j}}(-z)^{s_{j}-k_{j}})\otimes(\otimes_{j\neq i}x^{s_{j}-k_{j}}y^{k_{j}})\right){\otimes}\left((-z)^{s_{i}}\otimes 1\right)

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{(k_{j})_{j\neq i}}(\otimes_{j\neq i}\binom{s_{j}}{k_{j}}w^{k_{j}}(-z)^{s_{j}-k_{j}})\otimes(\otimes_{j\neq i}x^{s_{j}-k_{j}}y^{k_{j}})\right){\otimes}\left(-wz\otimes 1\right)

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{(k_{j})_{j\neq i}}(\otimes_{j\neq i}\binom{s_{j}}{k_{j}}w^{k_{j}}(-z)^{s_{j}-k_{j}})\otimes(\otimes_{j\neq i}x^{s_{j}-k_{j}}y^{k_{j}})\right){\otimes}\left(w^{s_{i}}\otimes 1\right)

Proof.

We use
Lemma

2.16
and
Corollary

2.8
to identify a non-trivial element of the one-dimensional space
H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}})^{\GL_{2}(k)}
. Writing the duals of the chosen basis of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
in terms of our chosen basis for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}

using
Lemma

2.11
, we find that the element is given by

A\otimes u^{2}+2B\otimes uv+C\otimes v^{2}

where
\{u^{2},uv,v^{2}\}
are the basis of
(\mathrm{det}^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{2}k^{2})\otimes_{k,\theta_{i}}\overline{\mathbf{F}}
induced from the standard basis of
k^{2}
denoted
\{u,v\}
and
A
,
B
and
C
are as in the statement of the Proposition. Using
Item

1
,
A\otimes u^{2}+2B\otimes uv+C\otimes v^{2}
is seen to be the cocycle
\kappa_{i}^{l}A+\epsilon_{i}B+\kappa_{i}^{u}C
.∎

Armed with descriptions of the groups flanking
H^{1}({\mathcal{K}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}})
in the left exact sequence
Equation

2.3
, our next order of business is to study the map denoted
\mathit{res}
.
Continuing with the setup of
Proposition

2.19
, we will now check if a
\GL_{2}(k)
-invariant cocycle in
H^{1}({{\mathcal{K}}}_{1},\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})
is in the image of the
\mathit{res}
map in
Equation

2.3
. Therefore, we will try and extend such a cocyle (necessarily factoring through
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}
by
Corollary

2.9
) to
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}
.

Theorem 2.20
.

Let
e=1
. Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be a pair of Serre weights as in the setup of
Proposition

2.19
. Further, let
s_{i+1}<p-1
. Then
\mathit{res}
is the zero map.

Proof.

We will assume without loss of generality that
t^{\prime}_{j}=0
for each
j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
. Let
U
be the group of upper unipotent matrices, and let
D
be the group of diagonal matrices with
1
in the bottom right entry. We identify
U
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
under the isomorphism

(2.21)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{K}

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\sim}U

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle\longmapsto U(\alpha):=\begin{pmatrix}1&\alpha\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

and
D
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
and
k^{\times}\times(1+\pi{\mathcal{O}}_{K})
under the isomorphisms

(2.22)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\qquad k^{\times}\times(1+\pi{\mathcal{O}}_{K})

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\qquad D

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\qquad\bar{t},t-[\bar{t}]

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\qquad D(t):=\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

where
\bar{t}
is the mod
\pi
reduction of
t
and
[\bar{t}]
is the Teichmüller lift of
\bar{t}
.

Let
W
be the
\langle U,D\rangle
-representation obtained by taking the quotient of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
by the
\langle U,D\rangle
-invariant
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-subspace spanned by those basis elements in
Equation

2.18
whose corresponding index
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k^{\prime}})
satisfies
k^{\prime}_{j}\neq 0
for some
j
. For
\mathbf{k}=(k_{j})_{j}\in\mathfrak{K}
satisfying
k_{j}\in[0,s_{j}]
for each
j
, we let
\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbf{k}}
denote the image of

\big((\otimes_{j\neq i}w^{k_{j}}z^{s_{j}-k_{j}})\otimes(\otimes_{j\neq i}y^{s_{j}})\big){\otimes}\big(w^{k_{i}}z^{s_{i}-k_{i}}\otimes y^{s_{i}-2}\big)

in
W
.
Evidently, the set
\{\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbf{k}}\}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathfrak{K}}
gives a basis of
W
.

Consider the cocycle
\kappa_{i}^{l}A+\epsilon_{i}B+\kappa_{i}^{u}C
defined in
Proposition

2.19
. Denote by
\psi
the cocycle obtained by restricting the domain to
\langle U,D\rangle\cap{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
and composing the codomain with the quotient map
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}\twoheadrightarrow W
.
Explicitly, since
\kappa_{i}^{l}
vanishes on
\langle U,D\rangle\cap{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
, the cocycle
\psi
is given by

(2.23)

\displaystyle\psi=-\epsilon_{i}\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbf{a}}+\kappa_{i}^{u}\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbf{b}},

where
\mathbf{a}=(a_{j})_{j}
and
\mathbf{b}=(b_{j})_{j}
satisfy
a_{i}=s_{i}-1
,
a_{j}=s_{j}
for all
j\neq i
and
b_{j}=s_{j}
for all
j
.

The proof of the theorem will follow if we show that there does not exist an extension of
\psi
to a
W
-valued cocycle defined on all of
\langle U,D\rangle
. Suppose, on the contrary that such an extension exists, denoted
q
. Denote by
q_{\mathbf{k}}
the coordinates of
q
corresponding to the basis vector
\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbf{k}}
.

We study first the cocycle
q|_{D}
. We note that for
t\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
,

D(t)\cdot\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbf{k}}=\theta_{f-1}(\bar{t})^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbf{k}}

where
\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}
is the unique integer in
[0,p^{f}-1)
that is equivalent mod
p^{f}-1
to

(2.24)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[0,f-1],\\
\not\equiv i\end{subarray}}p^{f-1-j}(k_{j}-s_{j})+p^{f-1-i}(k_{i}-s_{i}+1).

Thus, letting
\overline{\mathbf{F}}(\lambda_{\mathbf{k}})
denote a one-dimensional
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-vector space with action of
t\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
given by multiplication with
\theta_{f-1}(\bar{t})^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}
,

q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{D}:D\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}(\lambda_{\mathbf{k}})

is a cocycle for each
\mathbf{k}
. Suppose
\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}\neq 0
. An examination of the expression in
Equation

2.24
shows that this is equivalent to
k_{i}\neq s_{i}-1
or
k_{j}\neq s_{j}
for some
j\neq i
. This is in turn equivalent to
q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{D\cap{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}=0
, using
Equation

2.23
. Thus, under the identification of
D
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
in
Equation

2.22
,
q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{D}
can be viewed as a cocycle
k^{\times}\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}(\lambda_{\mathbf{k}})
. We claim that this cocycle is given by a coboundary. Indeed,

\sum\limits_{\xi\in k^{\times}}\xi^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}=0

because if
\xi_{0}
is the generator of the cyclic group
k^{\times}
,

\xi_{0}^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}\sum\limits_{\xi\in k^{\times}}\xi^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}=\sum\limits_{\xi\in k^{\times}}(\xi_{0}\xi)^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}=\sum\limits_{\xi\in k^{\times}}\xi^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}.

Hence,

H^{1}(k^{\times},\overline{\mathbf{F}}(\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}))\cong{\overline{\mathbf{F}}}/{(\theta_{f-1}(\xi_{0})^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}-1)\overline{\mathbf{F}}}=0.

Therefore, we can fix an element
e_{\mathbf{k}}\in\overline{\mathbf{F}}
such that

q_{\mathbf{k}}(D(t))=D(t)\cdot e_{\mathbf{k}}-e_{\mathbf{k}}.

Having thus defined
e_{\mathbf{k}}
when
\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}\neq 0
, we let
e_{\mathbf{k}}:=0
when
\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}=0
. Let
e\in W
be the vector whose
\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbf{k}}
-th coordinate is given by

e_{\mathbf{k}}
. By subtracting from
q
the coboundary

D(t)\mapsto D(t)\cdot e-e

if necessary, we may assume that
q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{D}=0
whenever
\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}\neq 0
. When
\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}=0
,
q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{D}:D\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}
is a group homomorphism. Since the order of
k^{\times}
is prime to
p
,
q_{\mathbf{k}}(D([\xi]))=0
for all
\xi\in k^{\times}
. Hence,
q([\xi])=0
for all
\xi\in k^{\times}
.

Next, we study
q|_{U}
. Suppose
\mathbf{k}\neq\mathbf{s}
. Then by
Equation

2.23
,
q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{U(\pi\mathcal{O}_{K})}=0
and
q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{U}
can be seen as a map on
k
. Since

D([\xi])U(\alpha)=U([\xi]\alpha)D([\xi])

for
\xi\in k^{\times}
,
\bar{\alpha}\in k
and
\alpha\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
any lift of
\bar{\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\theta_{f-1}(\xi)^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(\alpha))=q_{\mathbf{k}}(D([\xi])U(\alpha))=q_{\mathbf{k}}(U([\xi]\alpha)D([\xi]))=q_{\mathbf{k}}(U([\xi]\alpha)).

If
\bar{\alpha}=0
, then by direct calculation, and if
\bar{\alpha}\neq 0
, then by replacing
\alpha
with
1
and
\xi
with
\bar{\alpha}
in the equation above, we find that

(2.25)

\displaystyle\theta_{f-1}(\bar{\alpha})^{\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}}q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(1))=q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(\alpha)).

Next, we will show through an inductive argument that if
\mathbf{k}\neq\mathbf{s}
, then
q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{U}=0
. The argument will crucially use the following observation:
If
\mathbf{k}\neq\mathbf{s}
, then there does not exist
m\in[0,f-1]
such that
\lambda_{\mathbf{k}}=p^{f-1-m}
.
To see this, suppose on the contrary that

\sum\limits_{j\not\equiv i}p^{f-1-j}(k_{j}-s_{j})+p^{f-1-i}(k_{i}-s_{i}+1)\equiv p^{f-1-m}\mod p^{f}-1.

If
m\equiv i
mod
f
, then
k_{j}=s_{j}
for all
j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
, a contradiction. Now suppose
m\not\equiv i
mod
f
. Identifying
i
with its lift in
[0,f-1]
, if
i<m
(resp.
i>m
), there exists a largest
l\in[i+1,m]
(resp.
[i+1-f,m]
) such that viewing
l
as an element of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
,
s_{l}<p-1
. This is because
s_{i+1}<p-1
by hypothesis.
Thus, mod
p^{f}-1
,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l,m]\\
\mod f\end{subarray}}p^{f-1-j}s_{j}+p^{f-1-m}\equiv p^{f-1-l}(s_{l}+1).

Consequently,

\sum_{j}p^{f-1-j}k_{j}\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\not\in[l,m]\cup\{i\}\\
\mod f\end{subarray}}p^{f-1-j}s_{j}+p^{f-1-i}(s_{i}-1)+p^{f-1-l}(s_{l}+1),

implying that
k_{l}=s_{l}+1
, an impossibility as for each
j
,
k_{j}\in[0,s_{j}]
.

For each
n\geq 0
, we define a relation
<_{n}
on the set of indices
\mathbf{k}
by saying

\mathbf{m}=(m_{j})_{j}<_{n}\mathbf{k}=(k_{j})_{j}

if
m_{j}\leq k_{j}
for each
j
and
\sum_{j}(k_{j}-m_{j})=n
. Let
\mathbf{k}\not\in\{(0)_{j},\mathbf{s}\}
be such that if there exists
\mathbf{m}<_{n}\mathbf{k}
for some
n\geq 2
, then
q_{\mathbf{m}}|_{U}=0
. Then for each
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
,

\displaystyle q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(\alpha))+q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(1))+

\displaystyle\hskip 68.28644pt\sum_{\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}}\left(\left(\prod_{j}\binom{s_{j}-m_{j}}{k_{j}-m_{j}}\right)\theta_{f-1}(\bar{\alpha})^{\sum\limits_{j}p^{f-1-j}(k_{j}-m_{j})}q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(1))\right)

\displaystyle=q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(\alpha+1))

\displaystyle=q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(1+\alpha))

\displaystyle=q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(1))+q_{\mathbf{k}}(U(\alpha))+\sum_{\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}}\left(\left(\prod_{j}\binom{s_{j}-m_{j}}{k_{j}-m_{j}}\right)q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(\alpha))\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}}\left(\left(\prod_{j}\binom{s_{j}-m_{j}}{k_{j}-m_{j}}\right)\theta_{f-1}(\bar{\alpha})^{\sum\limits_{j}p^{f-1-j}(k_{j}-m_{j})}q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(1))\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}}\left(\left(\prod_{j}\binom{s_{j}-m_{j}}{k_{j}-m_{j}}\right)q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(\alpha))\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}}\left(\left(\prod_{j}\binom{s_{j}-m_{j}}{k_{j}-m_{j}}\right)\theta_{f-1}(\bar{\alpha})^{\lambda_{\mathbf{m}}}q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(1))\right)

(by
Equation

2.25
)

In particular, each element in the image of
\theta_{f-1}\colon k\hookrightarrow\mathbf{F}
satisfies the following polynomial in
x
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}}\left(\prod_{j}\binom{s_{j}-m_{j}}{k_{j}-m_{j}}\right)q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(1))x^{\lambda_{\mathbf{m}}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}}\left(\prod_{j}\binom{s_{j}-m_{j}}{k_{j}-m_{j}}\right)q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(1))x^{\sum\limits_{j}p^{f-1-j}(k_{j}-m_{j})}

If there exists an
\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}
such that
q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(1))
is non-zero, then this polynomial is nonzero because for each
\mathbf{m^{\prime}}<_{1}\mathbf{k}
, there exists a distinct
l(\mathbf{m^{\prime}})\in[0,f-1]
such that

\sum\limits_{j}p^{f-1-j}(k_{j}-m^{\prime}_{j})\equiv p^{f-1-l(\mathbf{m^{\prime}})}\mod p^{f}-1

while
\lambda_{\mathbf{m^{\prime}}}\not\equiv p^{f-1-l}
for any
l
, as noted earlier. Since a polynomial of degree less than
p^{f}-1
cannot have
|k|
distinct roots, this creates a contradiction. Therefore, for all
\mathbf{m}<_{1}\mathbf{k}
,
q_{\mathbf{m}}(U(1))=0
and by
Equation

2.25
,
q_{\mathbf{m}}|_{U}=0
.

Applying this argument to
\mathbf{k}
satisfying
(0)_{j}<_{1}\mathbf{k}
, we find that
q_{(0)_{j}}|_{U}=0
. Applying inductively to
\mathbf{k}
satisfying
(0)_{j}<_{n}\mathbf{k}
for
n\in[2,\sum_{j}s_{j}]
, we find that whenever
\mathbf{m}\neq\mathbf{s}
,
q_{\mathbf{m}}|_{U}=0
.

Finally, we come to the last leg of the proof. Because
q_{\mathbf{k}}|_{U}=0
for each
\mathbf{k}\neq\mathbf{s}
,

q_{\mathbf{s}}(U(\alpha+\beta))=q_{\mathbf{s}}(U(\alpha))+q_{\mathbf{s}}(U(\beta)).

Therefore
q_{\mathbf{s}}(U(p))=pq_{\mathbf{s}}(U(1))=0
. From
Equation

2.23
, we know that
q_{\mathbf{s}}|_{U\cap{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}=\kappa_{i}^{u}|_{U\cap{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}
. As
p
is the uniformizer of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
\kappa_{i}^{u}(U(p))\neq 0
, giving a contradiction.
∎

Theorem 2.26
.

Suppose
e>1
. Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be non-isomorphic Serre weights and let
\sigma
denote the
\GL_{2}(k)
-representation
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
. Then
\mathit{res}
is a surjective map.

Proof.

Since
e>1
, we have
p\in\pi^{2}\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\mathrm{ur}}
be the ring of integers for the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
in
K
. The natural quotient map
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}/\pi^{2}\to k
admits a splitting given by
k\cong\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\mathrm{ur}}/p\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{K}/\pi^{2}
. This induces a splitting of the exact sequence
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GL_{2}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

Therefore,
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\cong{\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\rtimes\GL_{2}(k)
.

Suppose
\psi
is a cocycle representing a nonzero element of
H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2},\sigma)^{\GL_{2}(k)}
. As
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}
action is trivial on
\sigma
,
H^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2},\sigma)^{\GL_{2}(k)}=Z^{1}({\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2},\sigma)^{\GL_{2}(k)}
. Invariance under
{\GL_{2}(k)}
-action means precisely that if
b\in\GL_{2}(k)
and
a\in{\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}
, then
b^{-1}\psi(a^{b})=\psi(a)
, where
a^{b}
denotes
bab^{-1}
.

We define a function
\delta
on
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}/{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\rtimes\GL_{2}(k)
by setting
\delta((a,b))
equal to
\psi(a)
. We claim that
\delta
is a cocyle, i.e.,
\delta((a,b)(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}))=\delta((a,b))+(a,b)\cdot\delta((a^{\prime},b^{\prime}))
. Evaluation of the left hand side gives us:

L.H.S.

\displaystyle=\delta((aa^{\prime b},bb^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\psi(aa^{\prime b})

\displaystyle=\psi(a)+\psi(a^{\prime b})

Evaluation of the right hand side gives us:

R.H.S.

\displaystyle=\psi(a)+(a,1)(1,b)\cdot\psi(a^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\psi(a)+(1,b)\cdot\psi(a^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\psi(a)+(1,b)\cdot((1,b^{-1})\cdot\psi(a^{\prime b}))

(as
\psi
is
\GL_{2}(k)
-invariant)

\displaystyle=\psi(a)+\psi(a^{\prime b})

\displaystyle=\text{L.H.S.}

This establishes that
\delta
is a cocyle with
\mathit{res}(\delta)=\psi
. Using
Corollary

2.9
, we conclude that the map
\mathit{res}
is a surjection. ∎

3.
Families of
G_{K}
-representations and stack dimensions

In this section, we study the relationship between families of representations and dimensions of closed substacks of
\mathcal{X}
.
We note first that there exists a map of sets

\displaystyle\mathit{rep}:\bigcup_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[G_{K}]}(\chi_{2},\chi_{1})\to|{\mathcal{X}}|

where the union in the domain is over pairs of
G_{K}
-characters
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\colon G_{K}\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}
and the map is given by sending the equivalence class of a short exact sequence
1\to\chi_{1}\to V\to\chi_{2}\to 1
to the representation
V
.

For
x\in\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}
, let

\mathrm{ur}_{x}\colon G_{K}\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}

be the character that factors through
G_{K}\twoheadrightarrow\Gal(k^{\text{sep}}/k)
mapping the geometric Frobenius to
x
. We say that two
G_{K}
-representations with
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-coefficients are unramified twists of each other if one can be obtained from the other by tensoring with
\mathrm{ur}_{x}
for some
x
.

Definition 3.1
.

A set
{\mathcal{F}}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
of isomorphism classes of two-dimensional
G_{K}
-representations with
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-coefficients is a “family” of representations if there exists a pair of
G_{K}
-characters

\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\colon G_{K}\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}

so that for each
V\in{\mathcal{F}}
, there exist
a,b\in\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}
such that
V=\mathit{rep}(\mathfrak{a})
for some
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[G_{K}]}(\chi_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b},\chi_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a})
.

To indicate the data of
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
in such a family, we will denote the family by
\mathcal{F}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}
. Note that the choice of
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
is non-unique, and in particular, can be changed by unramified twists.

Definition 3.2
.

We say that two families
\mathcal{F}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2}}
are
separated
if the set
{\mathcal{F}}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}\cup{\mathcal{F}}_{\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2}}
is not itself a family. Otherwise, we say that
\mathcal{F}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2}}
are not separated.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a closed substack of
{\mathcal{X}}
. A family
{\mathcal{F}}\subset|{\mathcal{E}}|
is said to be maximal in
{\mathcal{E}}
if any family
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\subset|{\mathcal{E}}|
that is not separated from
{\mathcal{F}}
satisfies
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{F}}
.

Consider
\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}=\Spec\mathbf{F}[x,x^{-1},y,y^{-1}]
as parametrizing the unramified twists of
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
in the following way:
If
p=(p(x),p(y))\in\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
, then
p
corresponds to the pair of characters
\chi_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{p(x)}
and
\chi_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{p(y)}
.

Definition 3.4
.

We say that a family
\mathcal{F}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}
is
of dimension
\leq d
(resp.
of dimension
d
)
if there exists a dense open subset
W
of
\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}
such that if
p\in W(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
, then

\{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[G_{K}]}(\chi_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{p(y)},\chi_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{p(x)})|\mathit{rep}(\mathfrak{a})\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}\}

is a subspace of dimension
\leq d
(resp. of dimension
d
).

Let
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a set of non-Steinberg weights for some indexing set
I
. We record a fact from
[
GLS
]
that we will use in this section. Suppose
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
are distinct
G_{K}
-characters with coefficients in
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
. Whenever non-empty, the set

(3.5)

\displaystyle L_{\sigma}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2}):=\{\mathfrak{a}\in\Ext^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[G_{K}]}(\chi_{2},\chi_{1})|\sigma\in W(\mathit{rep}(\mathfrak{a}))\}

is an
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-subspace of
\Ext^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[G_{K}]}(\chi_{2},\chi_{1})
.
Further, suppose
\chi^{\prime}_{1}
and
\chi^{\prime}_{2}
are unramified twists of
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
respectively. If
\chi^{\prime}_{1}\neq\chi^{\prime}_{2}
,

(3.6)

\displaystyle\dim_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}}\big(\bigcap_{i\in I}L_{\sigma_{i}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\big)=\dim_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}}\big(\bigcap_{i\in I}L_{\sigma_{i}}(\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2})\big)

and
if
\chi^{\prime}_{1}=\chi^{\prime}_{2}
, then

(3.7)

\displaystyle\dim_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}}\big(\bigcap_{i\in I}L_{\sigma_{i}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\big)+1=\dim_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}}\big(\bigcap_{i\in I}L_{\sigma_{i}}(\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2})\big).

3.1.
Scheme-theoretic families of
G_{K}
-representations

There exist finitely many
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-valued characters of
I_{K}
that admit extensions to
G_{K}
. Each such character is in fact valued in
\mathbf{F}
and is described uniquely by
a=(a_{i})_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}
with each
a_{i}\in[0,p-1]
and at least some
a_{i}<p-1
. Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be the set of such
f
-tuples indexed over
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
. Then for
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
, the corresponding
I_{K}
-character is given by

\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{a_{i}}.

Fix an extension of such a character to
G_{K}
, and denote it by
\psi_{a}\colon G_{K}\to\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. When each
a_{i}=0
, take
\psi_{a}=1
. When
a
satisfies

\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{a_{i}}=\epsilon|_{I_{K}}

where
\epsilon
is the mod
p
cyclotomic character, take
\psi_{a}
to be
\epsilon
.
By applying the functor
\mathbf{D}
defined in
[
EG2
, Sec. 3.6]
, we obtain a set of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules
\{\mathbf{D}(\psi_{a})\}_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}
with
\mathbf{F}
-coefficients. Recall that if
A
is an
\mathbf{F}
-algebra, a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
N
with
A
-coefficients is a module over the ring
\mathbf{A}_{K,A}
, defined in
[
EG2
, Sec. 2.2]
. We will say that
N
is defined over
A
or
\Spec A
. If there exists a map
f:\Spec B\to\Spec A
of
\mathbf{F}
-schemes, then we will denote by
N_{B}
or
N|_{\Spec B}
or
f^{*}N
the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module obtained by changing coefficients from
A
to
B
, that is, the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
N\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}\mathbf{A}_{K,B}
.
For étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
defined over schemes
\Spec A_{1}
and
\Spec A_{2}
respectively, we let
N_{1}\boxtimes N_{2}
denote the étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}N_{1}\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,A_{1}\otimes A_{2}}}\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}N_{2}
defined over
\Spec A_{1}\times\Spec A_{2}
where for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\mathrm{pr}_{i}\colon\Spec A_{1}\times\Spec A_{2}\to\Spec A_{i}
is the projection onto the
i
-th factor.

Let
M
be a rank
1
étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathbf{G}_{m}=\Spec\mathbf{F}[x,x^{-1}]
such that it is generated as a free module over
\mathbf{A}_{K,\mathbf{F}[x,x^{-1}]}
by
v\in M
with

\varphi(v)=xv

and trivial
\Gamma
-action. As described in
[
EG2
, Sec. 5.3]
, if
\alpha\in\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}
, then

\mathbf{D}(\mathrm{ur}_{\alpha})=p^{*}M

where
p:\Spec\overline{\mathbf{F}}\to\mathbf{G}_{m}
is the map which at the level of rings is given by mapping
x\mapsto\alpha
. In this sense,
M
interpolates the unramified characters of
G_{K}
.

For
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
, let
M_{a}
denote the étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbf{D}(\psi_{a})\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,\mathbf{F}}}M
defined over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
. Thus
M_{a}
interpolates the unramified twists of
\psi_{a}
.
Let
X_{a}=\mathbf{G}_{m}
when
\psi_{a}
is not trivial or cyclotomic, and let
X_{a}=\mathbf{G}_{m}\smallsetminus\{1\}=\Spec\mathbf{F}[x,x^{-1}][\frac{1}{x-1}]
when
\psi_{a}
is trivial or cyclotomic.

We now make some constructions following
[
EG2
, Sec. 5]
. For details refer to
loc. cit.

Extensions of a character by a generic character

The cohomology groups of
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(M_{a}|_{X_{a}})
, the Herr complex associated to
M_{a}|_{X_{a}}
, vanish in degrees
0
and
2
(the latter by Tate local duality). Following the arguments in
[
EG2
, Sec. 5.4]
, the cohomology group in degree
1
is compatible with arbitrary finite type base-change and gives a coherent sheaf on
X_{a}
. By the local Euler characteristic formula, the rank of this sheaf is the constant
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]
at every point. Thus, the reducedness of the base implies that it is a locally free sheaf. Denoting the total space of this sheaf by
V_{a}
, we find using
[
EG1
, Lem. 7.1.2]
that
V_{a}
parameterizes extensions of
\mathbf{D}(1)|_{X_{a}}
by
M_{a}|_{X_{a}}
. That is,
V_{a}
represents the functor that assigns to any
\mathbf{F}
-algebra
A
the data of isomorphism classes of pairs
f=(\underline{f},\mathfrak{a})
where
\underline{f}\colon\Spec A\to X_{a}
is a map and
\mathfrak{a}\in\Ext^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)/\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}(\mathbf{D}(1)_{A},\underline{f}^{*}M_{a}|_{X_{a}})
is an extension class.
For each
b\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we can consider

V_{a,b}:=V_{a}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}

as parameterizing extensions of
\mathbf{D}(1)|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b}
by

M_{a}|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b}
, étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules defined over
X_{a}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}
. That is, if
f=(\underline{f},\mathfrak{a})\in V_{a}(A)
and
g\in\mathbf{G}_{m}(A)
, then
(\underline{f},g)
gives a map
\Spec A\to X_{a}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}
and if
\mathfrak{a}
is the equivalence class of a short exact sequence

1\to\underline{f}^{*}M_{a}|_{X_{a}}\xrightarrow{\iota}E\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathbf{D}(1)_{A}\to 1,

then the extension class encoded by
(f,g)\in V_{a,b}(A)
is the equivalence class of the short exact sequence

1\to\underline{f}^{*}M_{a}|_{X_{a}}\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}g^{*}M_{b}\xrightarrow{\iota\otimes 1}E\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}g^{*}M_{b}\xrightarrow{\pi\otimes 1}\mathbf{D}(1)_{A}\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}g^{*}M_{b}\to 1.

Furthermore,
E\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}g^{*}M_{b}
gives an
A
-point of
{\mathcal{X}}
, giving rise to a morphism

f_{a,b}\colon V_{a,b}\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{X}

mapping an extension of a pullback of
\mathbf{D}(1)|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b}
by a pullback along the same morphism of

M_{a}|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b}
to the corresponding étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module.
There also exists a map

V_{a,b}\xrightarrow{\pi_{a,b}}B_{a,b}:=X_{a}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}

induced by the structure map
V_{a}\to X_{a}
and the identity map
\mathbf{G}_{m}\to\mathbf{G}_{m}
. On closed points, the map
\pi_{a,b}
corresponds to choices of unramified twists of
\psi_{a}
and
\psi_{b}
respectively.

As described in
[
EG1
, Sec. 7.3]
, there exists an action of
\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}
on extensions of
\mathbf{D}(1)|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b}
by
M_{a}|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b}
which induces a monomorphism

(3.8)

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}\times V_{a,b}\to V_{a,b}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}}V_{a,b}

given in the following way: Let
(r,s,f)
be an
A
-point of the domain such that
f
corresponds to the data of
\underline{f}\colon\Spec A\to B_{a,b}
and an extension class
\mathfrak{a}
represented by a short exact sequence

(3.9)

\displaystyle 1\to\underline{f}^{*}(M_{a}|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b})\xrightarrow{\iota}E\xrightarrow{\pi}\underline{f}^{*}(\mathbf{D}(1)|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b})\to 1.

Then, the image of
(r,s,f)
is

•

f
in the first coordinate,

•

rs^{-1}\cdot f
in the second coordinate (giving the action), where
rs^{-1}\cdot f
is defined to correspond to the data of the map
\underline{f}:\Spec A\to B_{a,b}
and the extension class
rs^{-1}\mathfrak{a}
, which is the equivalence class of the short exact sequence

(3.10)

\displaystyle 1\to\underline{f}^{*}(M_{a}|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b})\xrightarrow{r^{-1}s\iota}E\xrightarrow{\pi}\underline{f}^{*}(\mathbf{D}(1)|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b})\to 1,

and

•

the unique automorphism of
E
as a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module that induces the map from the sequence in
Equation

3.9
to that in
Equation

3.10
given by multiplication by
r
on
\underline{f}^{*}(M_{a}|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b})
and
s
on
\underline{f}^{*}(\mathbf{D}(1)|_{X_{a}}\boxtimes M_{b})
. The uniqueness of this automorphism is a consequence of the fact that by definition of
X_{a}
,
\mathbf{D}(1)
is not isomorphic to
M_{a}
after restriction to any field-valued point of
X_{a}
, and therefore, not isomorphic to
M_{a}
after any base change.

Note that
f_{a,b}(f)=E=f_{a,b}(rs^{-1}\cdot f)
, and so, along with the datum of the automorphism of
E
in the third bullet, we do indeed get a point of the stacky fiber product
V_{a,b}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}}V_{a,b}
.

Extensions of a character by itself

Next, we consider the Herr complex
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{D}(1))
associated to
\mathbf{D}(1)
defined over
\mathbf{F}
. Each of the cohomology groups is a finite-dimensional vector space over
\mathbf{F}
. As before, the considerations in
[
EG2
, Sec. 5.4]
show that for any finite type
\mathbf{F}
-algebra
R
,

H^{1}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{D}(1)_{R}))=H^{1}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{D}(1))\otimes R.

Thus
V_{1}=\Spec\Sym^{\bullet}(H^{1}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{D}(1))^{\vee})
parameterizes extensions of
\mathbf{D}(1)
by itself, and for each
b\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we can consider
V_{1,b}:=V_{1}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}
as parameterizing extensions of
\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b}
by itself.
Similar to the definition of
f_{a,b}
, we can define a map

f_{1,b}\colon V_{1,b}\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{X}

mapping extensions of pullbacks of
\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b}
by themselves to the corresponding
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules. The analogue of
\pi_{a,b}
in this setting is the map
\pi_{1,b}\colon V_{1,b}\to\mathbf{G}_{m}
which is simply projection onto the second factor.
Departing a little from our construction in
Equation

3.8
, we specify a monomorphism

(3.11)

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{a}\times V_{1,b}\to V_{1,b}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}}V_{1,b},

by first making a choice for a basis of the étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module corresponding to the map
f_{1,b}
.

The universal point of
V_{1,b}
corresponds to the map
\pi_{1,b}:V_{1,b}\to\mathbf{G}_{m}
and a universal extension of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
V_{1,b}
. Fix a representative short exact sequence of this universal extension:

1\to\pi_{1,b}^{*}(\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b})\xrightarrow{\iota}E\xrightarrow{\pi}\pi_{1,b}^{*}(\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b})\to 1,

where
E
is the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module corresponding to
f_{1,b}
.
Let
{\mathcal{O}}(V_{1,b})
be the ring of global functions on
V_{1,b}
. Since
\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b}
is projective as a
\mathbf{A}_{K,{\mathcal{O}}(V_{1,b})}
-module, the short exact sequence admits a splitting of
\mathbf{A}_{K,{\mathcal{O}}(V_{1,b})}
-modules. Fix such a splitting and using it, fix a basis of
E
as a rank
2
free
\mathbf{A}_{K,{\mathcal{O}}(V_{1,b})}
-module, with the first basis element being the image of a fixed free generator of
\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b}
under the inclusion
\iota
and the second basis element being the image of a fixed free generator of
\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b}
under the splitting of
\pi
. Denote the basis by
\{e_{1},e_{2}\}
. Suppose the action of
\varphi
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
with respect to this basis is given by upper triangular
2\times 2
matrices
M(\varphi)
and
M(\gamma)
respectively. Given a map
f\colon\Spec A\to V_{1,b}
, we can pull back along
f
to obtain an étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
f^{*}E
over
\Spec A
with a basis
\{f^{*}e_{1},f^{*}e_{2}\}
, and matrices
f^{*}M(\varphi)
and
f^{*}M(\gamma)
describing the action of
\varphi
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
on
f^{*}E
.

Armed with these choices, we now describe the map in
Equation

3.11
. Let
(r,s,b,f)
be an
A
-point of the domain. Suppose the map
f\colon\Spec A\to V_{1,b}
corresponds to the isomorphism class of a tuple
(\underline{f},\mathfrak{a})
, where
\underline{f}
is a map
\Spec A\to\mathbf{G}_{m}
and

\mathfrak{a}\in\Ext^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)/\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}(\mathbf{D}(1)_{A}\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}\underline{f}^{*}M_{b},\mathbf{D}(1)_{A}\otimes_{\mathbf{A}_{K,A}}\underline{f}^{*}M_{b})

is an extension class. Then the image of
(r,s,b,f)
is given by

•

f
in the first coordinate,

•

f^{\prime}=rs^{-1}\cdot f
in the second coordinate, where
rs^{-1}\cdot f
is defined to correspond to the tuple
(\underline{f},rs^{-1}\mathfrak{a})
, and

•

the datum of an isomorphism
f^{*}E\to f^{\prime*}E
with respect to the basis
\{f^{*}e_{1},f^{*}e_{2}\}
of the domain and
\{f^{\prime*}e_{1},f^{\prime*}e_{2}\}
of the codomain given by the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}r&b\\
0&s\end{pmatrix}.

Extensions of a character by its cyclotomic twist

Finally, we consider the Herr complex associated to
\mathbf{D}(\epsilon)
, denoted
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{D}(\epsilon))
. As before, viewing the finite-dimensional degree
1
cohomology group as an invertible sheaf on a point, the total space gives a vector bundle
V_{\epsilon}
defined over
\mathrm{Spec}\>\mathbf{F}
that parameterizes extensions of
\mathbf{D}(1)
by
\mathbf{D}(\epsilon)
.
For each
b\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we can consider
V_{\epsilon,b}:=V_{\epsilon}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}
as parameterizing extensions of
\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b}
by
\mathbf{D}(\epsilon)\boxtimes M_{b}
. Thus, we have a map

f_{\epsilon,b}\colon V_{\epsilon,b}\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{X}

given by sending an extension of a pullback of
\mathbf{D}(1)\boxtimes M_{b}
by a pullback along the same morphism of
\mathbf{D}(\epsilon)\boxtimes M_{b}
to the corresponding étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module. Let

\pi_{\epsilon,b}\colon V_{\epsilon,b}\to\mathbf{G}_{m}

be the projection onto the second factor.
Exactly as in the setting of
Equation

3.8
, there exists a monomorphism

(3.12)

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}\times V_{\epsilon,b}\to V_{\epsilon,b}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}}V_{\epsilon,b}

given in precisely the same way.

Irreducible representations

Next, we construct a version of the maps
f_{a,b}
,
f_{1,b}
and
f_{\epsilon,b}
to obtain the étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules corresponding to the irreducible mod
p
representations of
G_{K}
.
For each irreducible
2
-dimensional representation
\overline{\rho}
defined over a finite extension
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{F}
,
there exists a map
\mathbf{D}(\overline{\rho})\colon\Spec\mathbf{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
. In order to capture the unramified twists of
\overline{\rho}
, we construct a map

(3.13)

\displaystyle f_{\overline{\rho}}:\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{X}

corresponding to
\mathbf{D}(\overline{\rho})\boxtimes M
, where
M
is the rank
1
étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module we constructed earlier to interpolate the unramified characters of
G_{K}
. There exists a monomorphism

(3.14)

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}\times_{\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}}\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}

given by mapping a point
(r,s)
of the domain to
s
in both the first and second coordinates of the codomain and the automorphism of the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module corresponding to
f_{\overline{\rho}}\circ s
given by scalar multiplication by
r
.

Lemma 3.15
.

Suppose
\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}
is a finite extension of
\mathbf{F}
(resp. of
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
appearing in
Equation

3.14
), then
Equations

3.8
,
3.11
and
3.12
(resp.
Equation

3.14
) induce bijections on finite type points.

Proof.

This is easily verified on applying the functor
T_{\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}}
defined in
[
EG2
, Sec. 3.6]
to pass from projective étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules with
\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}
-coefficients to the equivalent category of
G_{K}
-representations with
\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}
-coefficients.
∎

Top-dimensional irreducible components in
\cap_{i}{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{i}}

Let
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a fixed set of non-Steinberg, pairwise non-isomorphic Serre weights. Let

\mathcal{E}:=\bigcap_{i\in I}\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{i}}.

By
[
CEGS
, Thm. 1.2]
, the set
|{\mathcal{E}}|
is precisely the set of isomorphism classes of
G_{K}
-representations

\{\overline{\rho}\colon G_{K}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})|\;\forall i\in I,\sigma_{i}\in W(\overline{\rho})\}.

From
Equation

3.6
, it follows that any maximal family of representations contained in
|{\mathcal{E}}|
has a well-defined dimension.

Theorem 3.16
.

Let
d\geq 0
. Suppose all maximal families of representations contained in
|\mathcal{E}|
are of dimension
\leq d
, and moreover,
|\mathcal{E}|
contains at least one maximal family of dimension
d
. Then the following are true:

(1)

\mathcal{E}
has dimension
d
.

(2)

If
d>0
, the number of
d
-dimensional components in
\mathcal{E}
equals the number of
d
-dimensional pairwise separated maximal families contained in
\mathcal{E}
.

(3)

Let
d=0
, and let

C:=\{\overline{\rho}\colon G_{K}\to\GL_{2}(\mathbf{F})\>|\>\overline{\rho}\text{ is semisimple}\}/\sim

where
\overline{\rho}\sim\overline{\rho}^{\prime}
if
\overline{\rho}
