and
\overline{\rho}^{\prime}
are isomorphic as
I_{K}
-representations. Then the number of
d
-dimensional components in
\mathcal{E}
equals
|C|
.

The proof of this proposition will be presented after
Proposition

3.21
. First, for each
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
, let
Y_{a,b}:=\mathcal{E}\times_{\mathcal{X}}V_{a,b}
. We also define
Y_{1,b}:=\mathcal{E}\times_{\mathcal{X}}V_{1,b}
and
Y_{\epsilon,b}:=\mathcal{E}\times_{\mathcal{X}}V_{\epsilon,b}
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
be fixed
G_{K}
-characters. Suppose
|\mathcal{E}|
contains a maximal family
\mathcal{F}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}
of representations of dimension
d
.
Let
a,b\in{\mathcal{A}}
be such that
\psi_{b}
is an unramified twist of
\chi_{2}
, while
\psi_{a}\otimes\psi_{b}
is an unramified twist of
\chi_{1}
. Then the scheme-theoretic image of
Y_{a,b}
under
f_{a,b}
has dimension
d
and the number of
d
-dimensional irreducible components in this scheme-theoretic image is exactly one.

Proof.

Let
q
be a closed point of
B_{a,b}
, and after fixing an embedding
\kappa(q)\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{F}}
, let
\overline{q}
be the corresponding
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-point of
B_{a,b}
. By
[
CEGS
, Thm. 1.2]
,
the set
(Y_{a,b})_{\overline{q}}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
equals

\bigcap_{i\in I}L_{\sigma_{i}}(\psi_{a}\otimes\psi_{b}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{\overline{q}(x)},\psi_{b}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{\overline{q}(y)})

and in particular, forms a vector space. By the construction of
B_{a,b}
, representations corresponding to the étale (
\varphi,\Gamma
)-modules obtained by pushing forward
Y_{a,b}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
under
f_{a,b}
are never an extension of a character by itself, and therefore, by
Equation

3.6
,
\dim_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}}(Y_{a,b})_{\overline{q}}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
does not depend on the choice of
q
or
\overline{q}
. Since
|\mathcal{E}|
contains a maximal family
\mathcal{F}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}
of representations of dimension
d
,
\dim_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}}(Y_{a,b})_{\overline{q}}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})=d
. This implies furthermore that
\pi_{a,b}(|Y_{a,b}|)=|B_{a,b}|
.

Since the
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-points of
(Y_{a,b})_{\overline{q}}
form a
d
-dimensional vector space, the reduced induced closed subscheme of
(Y_{a,b})_{q}
must be cut out by homogeneous linear equations in
V_{a,b}\times_{B_{a,b}}\kappa(q)
and thus be irreducible of dimension
d
.

Let
S
be an irreducible component of
Y_{a,b}
. Denote by
\overline{f_{a,b}(S)}
and
\overline{\pi_{a,b}(S)}
the scheme-theoretic images of
S
under
f_{a,b}
and
\pi_{a,b}
respectively. By
[
Sta
,
Tag 0DS4
]
, there exists a dense open
U\subset S
such that for any
p\in U(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
,

(3.18)

\displaystyle\dim\overline{f_{a,b}(S)}=\dim S-\dim_{p}(S_{f_{a,b}(p)})

and

(3.19)

\displaystyle\dim(\overline{\pi_{a,b}(S)})=\dim S-\dim_{p}(S_{\pi_{a,b}(p)}).

Fix
p\in U(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
. Since
(Y_{a,b})_{\pi_{a,b}(p)}
is irreducible, its reduced induced closed subscheme
((Y_{a,b})_{\pi_{a,b}(p)})^{\text{red}}
is contained entirely in some irreducible component of
Y_{a,b}
. Restrict
U
if necessary so that it is disjoint from all irreducible components of
Y_{a,b}
except
S
. We may thus assume that
((Y_{a,b})_{\pi_{a,b}(p)})^{\text{red}}\subset S
and
\dim(Y_{a,b})_{\pi_{a,b}(p)}=\dim S_{\pi_{a,b}(p)}=d
.

If
r\in\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}
, then
f_{a,b}(p)
and
f_{a,b}(r\cdot p)
are isomorphic
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules. Therefore,
r\cdot p\in V_{a,b}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
is also a point of
Y_{a,b}
.
Thus, there exists a monomorphism

(3.20)

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\kappa(p)\to Y_{a,b}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}}\kappa(p)\cong(Y_{a,b})_{f_{a,b}(p)}

induced by
Equation

3.8
, which is a bijection on finite type points as in
Lemma

3.15
. The existence of
Equation

3.20
uses the fact that the finite type points of the domain land in
(Y_{a,b})_{f_{a,b}(p)}
and the domain, being reduced, is the Zariski closure of its finite type points. The finite type points of
(Y_{a,b})_{f_{a,b}(p)}
are evidently contained in the finite type points of
((Y_{a,b})_{\pi_{a,b}(p)})_{f_{a,b}(p)}
, which in turn are contained in
S_{f_{a,b}(p)}
. Therefore the map in
Equation

3.20
can be factored as

\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\kappa(\mathfrak{a})\to S_{f_{a,b}(p)}\hookrightarrow(Y_{a,b})_{f_{a,b}(p)}

where both the arrows are monomorphisms and bijective on finite type points. Using
[
Sta
,
Tag 0DS4
]
, we infer that
\dim S_{f_{a,b}(p)}=2
.

We conclude from
Equations

3.18
and
3.19
that

\displaystyle\dim\overline{f_{a,b}(S)}=d-(2-\dim\overline{\pi_{a,b}(S)})\leq d

and

\dim\overline{f_{a,b}(S)}=d\iff\dim\overline{\pi_{a,b}(S)}=2.

Since
\pi_{a,b}(|Y_{a,b}|)=|B_{a,b}|
as noted earlier, for at least one of the finitely many irreducible components of
Y_{a,b}
, the scheme theoretic image under
\pi_{a,b}
has dimension
2
, showing that its scheme theoretic image under
f_{a,b}
has dimension
d
.

Next, we show that there exists exactly one irreducible component of
Y_{a,b}
whose scheme theoretic image under
\pi_{a,b}
has dimension
2
.
For
i\in\{1,2\}
, suppose
S^{i}
is an irreducible component of
Y_{a,b}
so that
\overline{\pi_{a,b}(S)}
has dimension
2
. Let
U^{i}
be the dense open subscheme of
S^{i}
obtained by taking the complement of all other irreducible components of
Y_{a,b}
. Therefore,
\overline{\pi_{a,b}(U^{i})}=\overline{\pi_{a,b}(S^{i})}=B_{a,b}
. Since
\pi_{a,b}(U^{i})
is constructible, it contains a dense open
W^{i}
of
B_{a,b}
. Let
W=W^{1}\cap W^{2}
. If
q
is a closed point of
W
, the irreducible scheme
((Y_{a,b})_{q})^{\operatorname{red}}
is contained entirely in at least one irreducible component of
Y_{a,b}
. But since for each
i
,
(U^{i})_{q}
is a non-empty locally closed subscheme of
(Y_{a,b})_{q}
that is disjoint from all irreducible components of
Y_{a,b}
apart from
S^{i}
,
S^{1}
must be the same as
S^{2}
.

Thus, there exists exactly one irreducible component of dimension
d
in the scheme-theoretic image of
Y_{a,b}
under
f_{a,b}
. ∎

Proposition 3.21
.

Suppose all maximal families of representations contained in
|\mathcal{E}|
have dimension
\leq d
. Then, for each
b\in{\mathcal{A}}
, the scheme theoretic images of
Y_{1,b}
and
Y_{\epsilon,b}
under
f_{1,b}
and
f_{\epsilon,b}
respectively have dimension strictly less than
d
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same ideas as the proof of
Proposition

3.17
. The reduction in dimension for the scheme-theoretic image of
f_{1,b}|_{Y_{1,b}}
arises from the fact that the codomain of
\pi_{1,b}:Y_{1,b}\to\mathbf{G}_{m}
has dimension
1
less than the codomain of
\pi_{a,b}
along with the fact that the fibers of the map
f_{1,b}|_{Y_{1,b}}
are
3
-dimensional, using
Equation

3.11
.

The reduction in dimension for the scheme-theoretic image of
f_{\epsilon,b}|_{Y_{\epsilon,b}}
arises from the fact that the codomain of the map
\pi_{\epsilon,b}:V_{\epsilon,b}\to\mathbf{G}_{m}
has dimension
1
less than the codomain of
\pi_{a,b}
.

We leave the details to the reader.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

3.16
.

By construction, each finite type point of
{\mathcal{E}}
corresponding to a reducible representation is in the image of one of the (finitely many)
f_{a,b}|_{Y_{a,b}}
,
f_{1,b}|_{Y_{1,b}}
and
f_{\epsilon,b}|_{Y_{\epsilon,b}}
maps. Further, each finite type point of
\mathcal{E}
corresponding to an irreducible representation is in the image of a map
f_{\overline{\rho}}
where
\overline{\rho}
is an irreducible representation. Since there are finitely many irreducible representations of
G_{K}
with
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-coefficients upto unramified twists, one can take a finite set of irreducible representations
I_{{\mathcal{E}}}
so that the underlying topological space of the union of the scheme-theoretic images of
f_{\overline{\rho}}
for
\overline{\rho}\in I_{{\mathcal{E}}}
contains all the irreducible representations in
|{\mathcal{E}}|
.
By an application of
[
Sta
,
Tag 0DS4
]
and using
Lemma

3.15
to calculate fiber dimension, the maps
f_{\overline{\rho}}
have irreducible scheme-theoretic images of dimension
0
. Thus,
\dim{\mathcal{E}}
is the maximum of the dimensions of the scheme-theoretic images of the various
f_{a,b}|_{Y_{a,b}}
,
f_{1,b}|_{Y_{1,b}}
and
f_{\epsilon,b}|_{Y_{\epsilon,b}}
maps.

The first statement then follows from
Propositions

3.17
and
3.21
, which also show that each
d
-dimensional family contains precisely one
d
-dimensional component in its closure.

Now assume that
\mathcal{Y}
is a
d
-dimensional irreducible component of
\mathcal{E}
contained in the closure of two
d
-dimensional maximal families
\mathcal{F}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2}}
. Then there exist unique
a,a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime}\in A
so that
(\psi_{a},\psi_{b})
and
(\psi_{a^{\prime}},\psi_{b^{\prime}})
are unramified twists of
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
and
(\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2})
respectively. Therefore,
\mathcal{Y}
is the scheme-theoretic image of an irreducible component
S
of
Y_{a,b}
as well as an irreducible component
S^{\prime}
of
Y_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}
.

Since the images of
|S|
and of
|S^{\prime}|
are constructible sets dense in
|\mathcal{Y}|
, there exists a dense open
V
of
|\mathcal{Y}|
contained in the image of both
|S|
and
|S^{\prime}|
. If
(a,b)\neq(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
, then this means that
(a,b)=(b^{\prime},a^{\prime})
and
V
contains only split reducible representations. Therefore, families of split reducible representations are dense in
\mathcal{Y}
.

Consider the closed subschemes
W
and
W^{\prime}
of
Y_{a,b}
and
Y_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}
respectively obtained by setting the extension classes to
0
. Clearly,
V
is in the images of both
|W|
and
|W^{\prime}|
, and therefore
{\mathcal{Y}}
is the scheme-theoretic image of both
W
and
W^{\prime}
under
f_{a,b}
and
f_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}
respectively. For each
\overline{q}\in B_{a,b}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
and
\bar{q}^{\prime}\in B_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
,
W_{\bar{q}}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
and
W^{\prime}_{\bar{q}^{\prime}}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
are
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
-vector space of dimension
0
. Thus, arguing exactly as in the proof of
Proposition

3.17
, for each closed point
q
of
B_{a,b}
and
q^{\prime}
of
B_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}
,
\dim W_{q}=\dim W^{\prime}_{q^{\prime}}=0
, and as a result, the scheme theoretic image of
W
under
f_{a,b}
, which is also the scheme-theoretic image of
W^{\prime}
under
f_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}
, has dimension
0
. If
d>0
, this is an impossibility as
{\mathcal{Y}}
has dimension
d
. This settles the second statement.

Finally, if
d=0
, then since
{\mathcal{E}}
doesn’t contain a family of dimension
>0
,
W=Y_{a,b}
and
W^{\prime}=Y_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}
. Maximality of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2}}
forces them to be the same family, that is uniquely identified by taking the isomorphism class of
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}}
where
\overline{\rho}
is any (reducible) point in this family. Next, suppose
\overline{\rho}^{\prime}
and
\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}
are irreducible
G_{K}
-representations defined over finite field extensions
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathbf{F}
respectively. Suppose, further, that the
0
-dimensional, irreducible scheme-theoretic image
{\mathcal{Y}}
of
f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime}}
is also the same as that of
f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}
. As before, since
f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime}}(|\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}|)
and
f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}(|\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}}|)
is constructible, there exists a dense open
V
in
|{\mathcal{Y}}|
which is contained in
f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime}}(|\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}|)\cap f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}(|\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}}|)
. Therefore,
\overline{\rho}^{\prime}
is an unramified twist of
\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}
, or equivalently,
\overline{\rho}^{\prime}\sim\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}
. On the other hand, by construction, if
\overline{\rho}^{\prime}\sim\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}
, then
f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime}}(|\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}|)=f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}(|\mathbf{G}_{m,\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}}|)
, showing that
f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime}}
and
f_{\overline{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}
have the same scheme-theoretic images. Using essentially the same argument, for any
a,b\in{\mathcal{A}}
and any irreducible
\overline{\rho}
, the scheme-theoretic image of any irreducible component of
Y_{a,b}
under
f_{a,b}
is necessarily not isomorphic to that of
f_{\overline{\rho}}
. This settles the third statement.
∎

4.
Serre weights of
G_{K}
-representations

In this section, the field of coefficients for all
I_{K}
and
G_{K}
-representations will be
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
, and we will omit the base field from the notations. If
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
are
G_{K}
-characters, then for any Serre weight
\sigma
, explicit recipes to compute the set
L_{\sigma}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
(defined in
Equation

3.5
) were provided by
[
DDR
]
in the unramified setting and later, by
[
Ste
]
in the general setting. We will recall these recipes
and compute conditions for
L_{\sigma}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
to equal the entire space of extensions or be of codimension
1
in it. When
K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, we will recall a criterion from
[
GLS
]
that determines when an irreducible representation
\overline{\rho}\colon G_{K}\to GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
satisfies
\sigma\in W(\overline{\rho})
.

4.1.
Serre weights of reducible representations

For this subsection, let

\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\colon I_{K}\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}

be fixed characters and let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
be a fixed Serre weight. Suppose
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
admit extensions to
G_{K}
-characters. This is equivalent to each of
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
being an exponent of
\omega_{i}
for some (any)
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
. Fix
G_{K}
-characters
\tilde{\chi}_{1}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{2}
satisfying
\tilde{\chi}_{1}|_{I_{K}}=\chi_{1}
,
\tilde{\chi}_{2}|_{I_{K}}=\chi_{2}
.

We note first that using Tate local duality and Euler characteristic formula,

\displaystyle\dim\Ext^{1}_{G_{K}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})=\begin{cases}[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]&\text{ if }\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}\not\in\{1,\epsilon\},\\
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]+1&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

where
\epsilon
denotes the mod
p
cyclotomic character.

Let
\overline{\rho}\colon G_{K}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
be a
G_{K}
-representation. Then
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}\in W(\overline{\rho})
if and only if
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}^{\vee}
is a Serre weight of
\overline{\rho}^{\vee}
in the sense of
[
GLS
,
DDR
,
Ste
]
. Therefore, our definition of
L_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})\subset\Ext^{1}_{G_{K}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
is not the same as the space denoted
L_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}
in
[
DDR
]
where the data of the
G_{K}
-characters
\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2}
is suppressed from the notation, or the space denoted
L_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
in
[
Ste
]
. To make a distinction, we will denote the aforementioned objects appearing in
[
DDR
,
Ste
]
by
L_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}^{*}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
instead. We have

(4.1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}\in L_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1},\tilde{\chi}_{1}^{-1})\iff\mathfrak{a}^{\vee}\in L_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}^{*}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})

where the extension class
\mathfrak{a}^{\vee}
is obtained by taking the equivalence class of the dual of a short exact sequence representing
\mathfrak{a}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

The set
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
is non-empty if and only if there exists a subset
J\subset\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
, and for each
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
there exists
x_{i}\in[0,e-1]
such that

(4.3)

\displaystyle\chi_{1}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}}\prod_{i\in J}\omega_{i}^{s_{i}+1+x_{i}}\prod_{i\not\in J}\omega_{i}^{x_{i}},\text{ and}

(4.4)

\displaystyle\chi_{2}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}}\prod_{i\in J}\omega_{i}^{e-1-x_{i}}\prod_{i\not\in J}\omega_{i}^{s_{i}+e-x_{i}}

Proof.

This is the content of the remark following
[
GLS
, Defn. 4.1.4]
.
∎

A basis of
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
using
[
Ste
]
.

Now, suppose
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})\neq\varnothing
. What follows is the recipe in
[
Ste
]
for writing down a basis of
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
, although we change the symbols used. Refer to
loc. cit.
for justifications of the statements and more details. We first write

(4.5)

\displaystyle\chi_{2}=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{m_{i}}

with each
m_{i}\in[0,p-1]
and not all
m_{i}
equal to
p-1
. This requirement uniquely determines
m_{i}
for each
i
.
Let
\mathcal{S}
be the necessarily non-empty set of
f
-tuples of non-negative integers
(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})
indexed over
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
and
satisfying

\chi_{2}=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{a_{i}}

and
a_{i}\in[0,e-1]\cup[s_{i}+1,s_{i}+e]
for all
i
.

For
i\neq f-1
, let
v_{i}
be the
f
-tuple
(0,\dots,0,-1,p,0,\dots,0)
with
-1
in
i
position,
p
in
i+1
position and
0
everywhere else. Let
v_{f-1}
be
(p,0,\dots,0,-1)
. Then there exists a subset
A\subset\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
such that

(4.6)

\displaystyle(m_{0},\dots,m_{f-1})+\sum_{i\in A}v_{i}\in\mathcal{S}

There exists a minimal subset of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
that is contained in any other subset
A\subset\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
satisfying
Equation

4.6
.

Definition 4.7
.

Define
A_{\min}
to be the minimal
A
satisfying
Equation

4.6
.

Definition 4.8
.

Given
(m_{0},\dots,m_{f-1})
and
A_{\min}
as above. Define

\displaystyle(y_{0},\dots,y_{f-1})

\displaystyle:=(m_{0},\dots,m_{f-1})+\sum_{i\in A_{\min}}v_{i}\in\mathcal{S}

and for each
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
,

\displaystyle z_{i}

\displaystyle:=s_{i}+e-y_{i}.

Remark 4.9
.

We have

\displaystyle\chi_{1}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{z_{i}},

\displaystyle\chi_{2}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{y_{i}},\>\text{ and}

\displaystyle\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{z_{i}-y_{i}}.

Definition 4.10
.

Define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{i}:=\begin{cases}[0,z_{i}-1]&\text{ if }y_{i}\geq s_{i}+1,\\
\{y_{i}\}\cup[s_{i}+1,z_{i}-1]&\text{ if }y_{i}<s_{i}+1.\end{cases}

Here, an interval
[a,b]\subset\mathbf{Z}
is interpreted to be empty if
b<a
.

Remark 4.11
.

When
e=1
,
\mathcal{I}_{i}=\{0\}
if
y_{i}=0
and
\mathcal{I}_{i}=\varnothing
if
y_{i}=s_{i}+1
.

Remark 4.12
.

Note that for each
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
,
|{\mathcal{I}}_{i}|\leq e
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{I}}_{i}|=e

\displaystyle\iff y_{i}=0.

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{I}}_{i}|=e-1

\displaystyle\iff\begin{cases}y_{i}=s_{i}+1,&\quad\text{ if }e=1,\\
y_{i}\in\{1,s_{i}+1\}&\quad\text{ if }e>1.\end{cases}

Definition 4.13
.

For each
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
, let

(4.14)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j=0}^{f-1}p^{j}(z_{i-j}-y_{i-j})\in\mathbf{Z}

(4.15)

\displaystyle\xi_{i}

\displaystyle:=(p^{f}-1)z_{i}+\lambda_{i}\in\mathbf{Z}

Suppose
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{a_{i}}
for
a_{i}\in[1,p]
and not all
a_{i}=p
.
We will extend the indices of the
a_{i}
from
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
to all of
\mathbf{Z}
by setting
a_{j}=a_{j^{\prime}}
if
j\equiv j^{\prime}\mod f
.
We call the tuple
(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})
the
tame signature
of
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}
. The arithmetic Frobenius element
\mathit{Frob}_{k}\in\Gal(k/\mathbf{F}_{p})
acts on
f
-tuples
(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})
via

(4.16)

\displaystyle\mathit{Frob}_{k}\cdot(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})=(a_{1},\dots,a_{f-1},a_{0}).

Let
f^{\prime}
be the cardinality of the orbit of
(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})
under the action of
\langle\mathit{Frob}\rangle
, and let
f^{\prime\prime}:=f/f^{\prime}
.

Let
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
and
u\in{\mathcal{I}}_{i}
. Let
\nu
be the
p
-adic valuation of
\xi_{i}-u(p^{f}-1)
. We let

(4.17)

\displaystyle\alpha(i,u):=(n,\kappa)\in\mathbf{Z}\times[0,f^{\prime\prime}-1]

where

(4.18)

\displaystyle n=\frac{\xi_{i}-u(p^{f}-1)}{p^{\nu}}

and
\kappa
is defined as follows: if
i_{n}\in[0,f^{\prime}-1]
is such that
i_{n}\equiv i-\nu\mod f^{\prime},
then

(4.19)

\displaystyle\kappa\equiv\frac{i-\nu-i_{n}}{f^{\prime}}\mod f^{\prime\prime}.

Definition 4.20
.

We define

J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2}):=\{\alpha(i,u)\>|\>i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z},u\in{\mathcal{I}}_{i}\}.

We emphasize that
J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
is defined if and only if
Equations

4.3
and
4.4
are true.

By
[
Ste
, Thm. 3.16]
, each
\alpha\in J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
specifies a unique element
c_{\alpha}
of a basis of
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
.
The space
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
is the span of

\{c_{\alpha}\>|\>\alpha\in J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\}

together with additional, distinguished basis elements

c_{\text{un}}
if
\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}=1
, and

c_{\text{tr}}
if
\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}=\epsilon
,
\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega^{-t_{i}}\otimes\chi_{2}=1
and
s_{i}=p-1
for all
i
.
Thus, whenever
\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}\not\in\{1,\epsilon\}
,

(4.21)

\displaystyle\dim L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})=|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}|\mathcal{I}_{i}|.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
be another Serre weight, not isomorphic to
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
. If
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
is also non-empty, then
a basis for
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})\cap L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
is given by

(4.22)

\displaystyle\{c_{\alpha}\>|\>\alpha\in J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\cap J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\}

together with
c_{\text{un}}
if
\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}
is trivial. The basis element
c_{\text{tr}}
doesn’t come into the picture because if it was contained in
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})\cap L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
, then
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
would be forced to be isomorphic, a contradiction.
In particular, whenever
\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}\not=1
,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\dim L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})\cap L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})=|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\cap J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|.

Proposition 4.24
.

Suppose
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
is non-Steinberg. Then

|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef

if and only if

\displaystyle\chi_{1}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{s_{i}+e}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}},\text{ and}

(4.25)

\displaystyle\chi_{2}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}}.

Proof.

By
Equation

4.21
and
Remark

4.12
,
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef
if and only if for each
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
,

|{\mathcal{I}}_{i}|=e\iff y_{i}=0.

Thus,
Remark

4.9
shows that
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef
implies
Proposition

4.24
. On the other hand, if
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
satisfy
Proposition

4.24
, then
A_{\mathrm{min}}
is seen to be the empty set and we obtain
y_{i}=0
for each
i
as desired.
∎

Definition 4.26
.

If
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
is non-Steinberg and the conditions in
Proposition

4.24
are satisfied, we say that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},{\mathbf{s}}}
is the
highest weight
for the pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
.

Note that because of the requirement that the highest weight be non-Steinberg, the highest weight for any pair of
G_{K}
-characters is well defined. Moreover, the highest weight only depends on the restrictions of the
G_{K}
-characters to
I_{K}
.

Proposition 4.27
.

Suppose
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
is non-Steinberg. Then

|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(1)

There exists
j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
such that

(4.28)

\displaystyle\chi_{1}=\omega_{j}^{e-1}\prod_{i\neq j}\omega_{i}^{s_{i}+e}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}},\qquad\chi_{2}=\omega_{j}^{s_{j}+1}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}},

and

(4.29)

\displaystyle s_{j}\leq\begin{cases}p-3\text{ if }f=1,\\
p-2\text{ if }f>1.\end{cases}

(2)

e=1,f>1
and there exists
j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
such that
Equation

4.28
holds,

s_{j-1}\neq 0
and
s_{j}=p-1
.

(3)

e>1
and there exists
j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
such that

(4.30)

\displaystyle\chi_{1}=\omega_{j}^{s_{j}+e-1}\prod_{i\neq j}\omega_{i}^{s_{i}+e}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}},\qquad\chi_{2}

\displaystyle=\omega_{j}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}}

and
s_{j}\neq 0
.

Proof.

By
Equation

4.21
and
Remark

4.12
,
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1
if and only there exists
j\in\mathbf{Z}/fZ
so that

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{I}}_{i}|=\begin{cases}e&\text{ if }i\neq j,\\
e-1&\text{ if }i=j.\end{cases}

which happens if and only if
either

(4.31)

\displaystyle y_{i}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }i\neq j,\\
s_{i}+1&\text{ if }i=j,\end{cases}

or,

\displaystyle e>1\text{ and}

(4.32)

\displaystyle y_{i}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }i\neq j,\\
1&\text{ if }i=j.\end{cases}

Note that
Equation

4.31
implies
Equation

4.28
, but the conditions on
e
and
s_{i}
’s in
Items

1
and
2
are not obvious. Similarly, when
Section

4.1
is true, then
Equation

4.30
is satisfied, but the condition
s_{j}\neq 0
in
Item

3
is not clear.

To see how the extra conditions arise, suppose first that
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
satisfy
Equation

4.28
. We need to compute
m_{i}
’s,
A_{\mathrm{min}}
and
y_{i}
’s and check when
y_{i}
’s satisfy either
Equation

4.31
or
Section

4.1
. We consider three cases:

•

If
s_{j}\leq p-2
with
f>1
or
s_{j}\leq p-3
with
f=1
, then
m_{i}=0
for
i\neq j
and
m_{j}=s_{j}+1
. Thus, the tuple
(m_{0},\dots,m_{f-1})
is in
{\mathcal{S}}
implying
A_{\mathrm{min}}=\varnothing
. This implies further that
y_{i}=m_{i}
for each
i
and
Equation

4.31
holds.

•

If
s_{j}=p-1
(with
f>1
, since
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
is assumed to be non-Steinberg), then
m_{i}=0
for
i\neq j-1
and
m_{j-1}=1
. We can obtain the desired values of
y_{i}
’s only if
(m_{0},\dots,m_{f-1})\not\in{\mathcal{S}}
which happens if and only if
e=1
and
s_{j-1}\neq 0
. When that happens,
A_{\mathrm{min}}=\{j-1\}
and we obtain
Equation

4.31
.

•

If
s_{j}=p-2
with
f=1
, then
m_{j}=0
. Therefore,
A_{\mathrm{min}}=\varnothing
and
y_{j}=0
, contradicting both
Equation

4.31
and
Section

4.1
.

Now, suppose
e>1
and
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
satisfy
Equation

4.30
but not
Equation

4.28
. Thus, we assume
s_{j}\neq 0
simply to avoid redundancy with
Items

1
and
2
. In this setting,
m_{i}=0
if
i\neq j
and
m_{j}=1
. Evidently,
(m_{0},\dots,m_{j})\in{\mathcal{S}}
. Therefore,
y_{i}=m_{i}
for each
i
and
Section

4.1
holds, but
Equation

4.31
is false.
∎

A basis of
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
using
[
DDR
]
.

When
e=1
,
[
DDR
]
provides an algorithm to specify a basis of
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
, assuming it to be non-empty. It will be significantly easier to use this algorithm for some of the calculations in the unramified case, and so, we recall some essentials of this algorithm starting with a few definitions, modified slightly where necessary to be consistent with our conventions. As before, let
(a_{0},...,a_{f-1})
denote the tame signature of
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}
with the indices of the
a_{i}
extended from
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
to all of
\mathbf{Z}
in the usual way. Consider the function

\delta:\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{Z}

defined as follows: For
j\in\mathbf{Z}
,
\delta(j)=j
unless there exists a (necessarily unique) integer
i>j
such that for each
k\in[j,i-2]
(taken to be the empty interval if
j=i-1
]),
a_{k}=p-1
and
a_{i-1}=p
. When this exception happens,
\delta(j)=i
. The function
\delta
induces a function
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
, also denoted by
\delta
.

Next, consider a function
\mu
on subsets of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
defined as follows:
If
\delta(J)\subset J
,
\mu(J):=J
. Else, choose some
i_{1}\in\mathbf{Z}
so that
\bar{i_{1}}\in\delta(J)\smallsetminus J
and let
j_{1}
be the largest integer such that
j_{1}<i_{1}
,
\bar{j_{1}}\in J
and
\delta(j_{1})=i_{1}
. If
J=\{\bar{j_{1}},\dots,\bar{j_{r}}\}
with
j_{1}>j_{2}>...>j_{r}>j_{1}-f
, define
i_{\kappa}
for
\kappa\in[2,r]
inductively as follows:

\displaystyle i_{\kappa}=\begin{cases}\delta(j_{\kappa})\>&\text{ if }i_{\kappa-1}>\delta(j_{\kappa}),\\
j_{\kappa}\>&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

Then
\mu(J):=\{\bar{i_{1}},\dots,\bar{i_{r}}\}
. Note, in particular, that the definitions of the functions
\delta
and
\mu
depend on
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}
.

Next, let

(4.33)

\displaystyle J_{\mathrm{max}}:=\{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}\;|\;y_{i}=0\}

where
y_{i}
are as defined in
Definition

4.8
.

When
e=1
, each
\alpha\in\mu(J_{\mathrm{max}})
specifies a distinguished basis element of
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
and if
\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}\not\in\{1,\epsilon\}
, then by
[
DDR
, Conj. 7.2]
and the main result of
[
CEGM
]
, these distinguished basis elements labelled by elements of
\mu(J_{\mathrm{max}})
give a complete basis of
L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
. Thus, assuming
e=1
,

|J^{\text{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=|J_{\mathrm{max}}|=|\mu(J_{\mathrm{max}})|.

Further, the constructions of
[
DDR
,
Ste
]
imply that if
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
is another non-Steinberg Serre weight not isomorphic to
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
, then

(4.34)

\displaystyle|J^{\text{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\cap J^{\text{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=|\mu(J_{\mathrm{max}})\cap\mu(J^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}})|

where
J^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}
is the analogue of
J_{\mathrm{max}}
for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
.

4.2.
Serre weights of irreducible
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-representations

When
K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, we will need results on Serre weights of irreducible representations.
Let
\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}
be the quadratic unramified extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
in its fixed algebraic closure with residue field
\mathbf{F}_{p^{2}}
. Fix a
(p^{2}-1)
-th root of
p
in the algebraic closure of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, denoted
\sqrt[p^{2}-1]{p}
, and consider the map

\displaystyle I_{K}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbf{F}_{p^{2}}^{\times}

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\longmapsto\frac{g(\sqrt[p^{2}-1]{p})}{\sqrt[p^{2}-1]{p}}\mod p.

On composing with the two distinct embeddings of
\mathbf{F}_{p^{2}}
into
\overline{\mathbf{F}}
, we obtain two characters
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}:I_{K}\to\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}
. These are the fundamental characters of level
2
.

Lemma 4.35
.

Let
\overline{\rho}\colon G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{F}})
be an irreducible representation. Then
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}\in W(\overline{\rho}^{\vee})
if and only if

\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}}\cong\eta_{1}^{t+s+1}\eta_{2}^{t}\oplus\eta_{1}^{t}\eta_{2}^{t+s+1}

Proof.

This is immediate from
[
GLS
, Defn. 4.1.4, Thm. 4.1.6]
keeping in mind that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}\in W(\overline{\rho}^{\vee})
if and only if
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}\in W^{\text{explicit}}(\overline{\rho})=W^{\text{cris}}(\overline{\rho})
in the sense of
[
GLS
]
.
∎

Proposition 4.36
.

Let
K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
and let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be non-isomorphic non-Steinberg Serre weights. The stack
{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}
has finite type points corresponding to irreducible representations if and only if
s^{\prime}=p-1-s
and
t^{\prime}\equiv t+s\mod p-1
.

Proof.

By
[
CEGS
, Thm. 1.2]
and
Lemma

4.35
, we need to determine when

\eta_{1}^{t+s+1}\eta_{2}^{t}\oplus\eta_{1}^{t}\eta_{2}^{t+s+1}=\eta_{1}^{t^{\prime}+s^{\prime}+1}\eta_{2}^{t^{\prime}}\oplus\eta_{1}^{t^{\prime}}\eta_{2}^{t^{\prime}+s^{\prime}+1}.

Taking ratios of the direct summands, we have two possibilities. Either

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{s+1}\eta_{2}^{-(s+1)}\quad

\displaystyle=\quad\eta_{1}^{s^{\prime}+1}\eta_{2}^{-(s^{\prime}+1)}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{s}\eta_{2}^{s^{\prime}}\quad

\displaystyle=\quad\eta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}\eta_{2}^{s}

or

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{s+1}\eta_{2}^{-(s^{\prime}+1)}\quad

\displaystyle=\quad\eta_{1}^{-(s^{\prime}+1)}\eta_{2}^{s+1}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{s}\eta_{2}^{p-s^{\prime}-1}\quad

\displaystyle=\quad\eta_{1}^{p-s^{\prime}-1}\eta_{2}^{s}.

Since
s,s^{\prime}<p-1
, the first case forces
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}\cong\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
, a contradiction. The second case implies the desired relationship between
(t,s)
and
(t^{\prime},s^{\prime})
.
∎

Remark 4.37
.

The criterion in the statement of
Proposition

4.36
is the same as that in
Proposition

2.4
1
1(b)
.

4.3.
Types of codimension one intersections

We combine the results of
Section

3
and this section to obtain the following theorem, true for arbitrary
K
.

Theorem 4.38
.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
be non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights. Then
{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}
has dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
if and only if one of the following is satisfied.

(1)

There exist
I_{K}
-characters
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
such that

(4.39)

\displaystyle|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\cap J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1.

(2)

K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
and

(4.40)

\displaystyle s^{\prime}=p-1-s,\qquad t^{\prime}\equiv t+s\mod p-1.

Let
n
be the number of irreducible components in
{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}
of dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
. If
K\neq\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, then
n
is the number of isomorphism classes of ordered pairs of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
satisfying
Item

1
. If
K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, then
n
equals the number of isomorphism classes of
I_{K}
-representations
\chi_{1}\oplus\chi_{2}
, where
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
satisfy
Item

1
, plus
1
if
Equation

4.40
holds.

Proof.

The existence of
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
satisfying
Item

1

is equivalent to the existence of
G_{K}
-characters
\tilde{\chi}_{1}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{2}
, so that for all
a,b\in\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}
such that

\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b^{-1}a}\neq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\dim\;L_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}^{-1}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b^{-1}},\tilde{\chi}^{-1}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a^{-1}})\cap L_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\tilde{\chi}^{-1}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b^{-1}},\tilde{\chi}^{-1}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a^{-1}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{notation-dual}}}{{=}}\>

\displaystyle\dim\;L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a},\tilde{\chi}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b})\cap L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a},\tilde{\chi}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{dim-L-cap}}}{{=}}\>

\displaystyle ef-1.

Thus, by
[
CEGS
, Thm. 1.2]
,
Item

1
is equivalent to the existence of a maximal family of representations
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\chi}^{-1}_{2},\tilde{\chi}^{-1}_{1}}
of dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
inside
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}|
. Clearly, when
K\neq\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, the number of pairwise separated maximal families of dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
in
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}|
equals the number of isomorphism classes of ordered pairs of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
satisfying
Item

1
.
Further, when
K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
,
Item

2
is equivalent to the existence of points corresponding to irreducible representations in
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}|
by
Proposition

4.36
. All such points corresponding to irreducible representations have the same isomorphism class after restricting to
I_{K}
by
Lemma

4.35
. An application of
Theorem

3.16
finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 4.41
.

We say that an unordered pair of non-isomorphic non-Steinberg Serre weights
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
have a “type I” intersection witnessed by an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
if after exchanging
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
if necessary,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
is the highest weight for
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
while
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1
.

Proposition 4.42
.

If
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
have a type I intersection, then
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\cap J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1
.

Proof.

After exchanging
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
if necessary, we have

|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef.

Therefore, there exist
G_{K}
-characters
\tilde{\chi}_{1}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{2}
, so that for all
a,b\in\overline{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}
such that
\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b^{-1}a}\not\in\{1,\epsilon\}
,

L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a},\tilde{\chi}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b})=\Ext^{1}_{G_{K}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a},\tilde{\chi}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b})
. As a result,

\displaystyle|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\cap J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\dim L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a},\tilde{\chi}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b})\cap L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a},\tilde{\chi}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b})

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\dim L^{*}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{a},\tilde{\chi}_{2}\otimes\mathrm{ur}_{b})

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1.

∎

Proposition 4.43
.

The Serre weights
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
can have a type I intersection witnessed by at most two pairs of
I_{K}
-characters.

Proof.

We have two possibilities, either
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef
or
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef
. In both cases,
Proposition

4.24
determines
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
completely.
∎

Definition 4.44
.

We say that an unordered pair of non-isomorphic non-Steinberg Serre weights
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
have a “type II” intersection witnessed by an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
if
J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})=J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
of cardinality
ef-1
.

Note that by definition of types and
Theorem

4.38
, the stack
{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}
has dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
if and only if
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
have either a type I intersection or a type II intersection.

Proposition 4.45
.

Suppose
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
have a type II intersection witnessed by
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
. Then there exists a unique non-Steinberg Serre weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
such that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
, as well as
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
, have a type I intersection witnessed by
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
. Moreover,
{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}}
has dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
. On the other hand, if there exist pairwise non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
satisfying
\dim{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}}=[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
, then at least two of
\{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}\}
have a type II intersection between them.

Proof.

Suppose first that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
have a type II intersection witnessed by
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
. Existence of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
such that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
, as well as
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
, have a type I intersection witnessed by
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
is equivalent to
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
being the highest weight for
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
. This in turn is equivalent to finding a solution for the equations

\displaystyle\chi_{1}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{i}+e}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t^{\prime\prime}_{i}},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\chi_{2}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t^{\prime\prime}_{i}}.

where each
s^{\prime\prime}_{i}\in[0,p-1]
and not all
s^{\prime\prime}_{i}
equal
p-1
. Such a solution clearly exists and is unique, settling the first part of the statement.

By the proof of
Theorem

4.38
,
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}|
contains a maximal family
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1},\tilde{\chi}_{1}^{-1}}
of dimension
ef-1
. Using
[
CEGS
, Thm. 1.2]
and similar arguments as in
Proposition

4.42
, we find that
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1},\tilde{\chi}_{1}^{-1}}
is contained in
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}}|
. An application of
Theorem

3.16
finishes the proof of the first half of the statement.

Now suppose
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
are pairwise non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights such that
\dim{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}}=[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
. Then by
Theorem

3.16
, there exists at least one maximal family of representations
{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi_{2}^{-1},\psi_{1}^{-1}}
of dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
in
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}}|
, where
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
are
G_{K}
-characters. Therefore, for any pair of non-isomorphic Serre weights in
\{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}\}
,
(\psi_{1}|_{I_{K}},\psi_{2}|_{I_{K}})
witnesses either a type I or a type II intersection. As there can be only one highest weight for
(\psi_{1}|_{I_{K}},\psi_{2}|_{I_{K}})
, at least one of these is a type II intersection.
∎

5.
Type I intersections

In this section and the next, we will explicitly determine the existence of type I and type II intersections between pairs of Serre weights. First, we introduce some more notation and conventions.
When comparing
f
-tuples
\mathbf{s}
and
\mathbf{s^{\prime}}
, we will often and without additional comment take the indices of the components of
\mathbf{s}
and
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
to be integers valued in
[0,f-1]
instead of element of the set
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
, by identifying each element of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
with its representative in
[0,f-1]
. The chosen set of indices,
[0,f-1]
versus
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
, will be clear from the context. While making a comparison, we will only state the values of
s_{i}
and
s^{\prime}_{i}
that have specific constraints or are potentially different from each other. If we say
(\dots,s_{i},\dots)=(\dots,\in[a,b],\dots)
or
(\dots,s_{i},\dots)=(\dots,c,\dots)
, we mean respectively that
s_{i}
can take any value
\in[a,b]
or
s_{i}=c
.
If for some
a,b\in[0,p-1]
and
j\in[-1,f-2]
, a subsequence
(s_{i},\dots,s_{i+j})
is set to equal
(a,\dots,a)
and
(s^{\prime}_{i},\dots,s^{\prime}_{i+j})
is set to equal
(b,\dots,b)
, then this means that for each
k\in[i,i+j]

s_{k}=a
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=b
. Here, the interval
[i,i+j]
is to be interpreted to be the empty set if
j=-1
.
If the values of
s_{i}
and
s^{\prime}_{i}
are not specified, then we assume that
s_{i}
can take any value in
[0,p-1]
and
s^{\prime}_{i}=s_{i}
. Similar notational norms apply with the roles of
s_{i}
and
s^{\prime}_{i}
interchanged. When
f=1
, we will omit the indices
i
in the tuples, and also write the character
\omega_{i}
as simply
\omega
.

We will retain the symbols
m_{i}
,
y_{i}
,
z_{i}
,
\mathcal{I}_{i}
,
\lambda_{i}
,
\xi_{i}
,
\alpha
and
J_{\mathrm{max}}
as defined in
Equation

4.5
,
Definitions

4.8
,
4.10
and
4.13
,
Equations

4.17
and
4.33
for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
, and will replace them respectively with
m^{\prime}_{i},y^{\prime}_{i}
,
z^{\prime}_{i}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{i}
,
\lambda^{\prime}_{i}
,
\xi^{\prime}_{i}
,
\alpha^{\prime}
and
J^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}
for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
and with
m^{\prime\prime}_{i},y^{\prime\prime}_{i}
,
z^{\prime\prime}_{i}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}_{i}
,
\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{i}
,
\xi^{\prime\prime}_{i}
,
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
and
J^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}
for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime\prime}}}
.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights. We will now compute criteria for existence of an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
witnessing a type I intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
.
As we saw in
Proposition

4.27
,
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1
can happen in one of three ways.

5.1.
Type I intersections when
f=1

5.1.1.
Case 1:
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
satisfies
Proposition

4.27
1
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
f=1
. There exists an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and the statement of
Proposition

4.27
1
holding true for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
(in particular,
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1
) if and only if

(5.2)

\displaystyle s^{\prime}=p-s-3\qquad\text{ and }\qquad t^{\prime}\equiv t-s^{\prime}-1\mod p-1.

This in turn happens if and only if
Proposition

2.4
1
1(a)
is true.

Proof.

Existence of such a pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\chi_{1}=\omega^{e-1+t^{\prime}}=\omega^{s+e+t},\qquad\chi_{2}=\omega^{s^{\prime}+1+t^{\prime}}=\omega^{t}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad s^{\prime}\leq p-3.

Comparing two different ways of writing
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}
, we obtain the following equivalences mod
p-1
:

\displaystyle s+e

\displaystyle\equiv e-2-s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\iff s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv-2-s\equiv p-3-s.

If
s=p-2
, then
s^{\prime}=p-2
, a contradiction. Therefore,
s\leq p-3
and
s=p-s^{\prime}-3
. Comparing the expressions for
\chi_{2}
, we get the desired expression for
t^{\prime}
.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

The criterion in
Equation

5.2
is symmetric in
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
. Therefore, when it holds, there exist two distinct ordered pairs of
I_{K}
-characters,
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
(\chi^{\prime}_{1},\chi^{\prime}_{2})
with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
, both witnessing type I intersections between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
.

When
K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, we get

\chi_{1}=\omega^{s+1+t}=\omega^{t^{\prime}}=\chi^{\prime}_{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\chi_{2}=\omega^{t}=\omega^{s^{\prime}+t^{\prime}+1}=\chi_{1}.

Therefore,
\chi_{1}\oplus\chi_{2}\cong\chi^{\prime}_{1}\oplus\chi^{\prime}_{2}
.

5.1.2.
Case 2:
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
satisfies
Proposition

4.27
3
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
f=1
and
e>1
. There exists an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and the statement of
Proposition

4.27
3
holding true for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
(in particular,
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1
) if and only if

\displaystyle s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}s+2&\text{ and }s<p-3,\text{ or }\\
1&\text{ and }s=p-2,\end{cases}

(5.5)

\displaystyle t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv t-1\mod p-1

If the above holds, then if
s<p-3
, then
Proposition

2.14
is true, and if
s=p-2
, then
Proposition

2.4
1
1(b)
is true.

Proof.

Existence of such a pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\chi_{1}=\omega^{s^{\prime}+e-1+t^{\prime}}=\omega^{s+e+t},\qquad\chi_{2}=\omega^{1+t^{\prime}}=\omega^{t}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad s^{\prime}\neq 0.

Comparing the two ways of writing
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}
, we obtain the following equivalence mod
p-1

\displaystyle s+e

\displaystyle\equiv e-2+s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\iff s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv s+2.

Thus, the requirement that
s^{\prime}\neq 0
gives the conditions on
s
and
s^{\prime}
as desired. The expression for
t^{\prime}
follows from the expressions for
\chi_{2}
.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

The criteria in
Proposition

5.4
are not symmetric when
p>3
and symmetric when
p=3
. Therefore, if these criteria hold and
p>3
(resp.
p=3
), then there exists exactly one (resp. two) ordered pair(s) of
I_{K}
-characters witnessing a type 1 intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
.

5.2.
Type I intersections when
f>1

5.2.1.
Case 1:
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
satisfies
Proposition

4.27
1
or
2
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
f>1
. There exists an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and the statement of
Proposition

4.27
1
or
2
holding true for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
(in particular,
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1
) if and only if, after translating the indices for the components of
\mathbf{t}
,
\mathbf{s}
,
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
by adding a common fixed element of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
if necessary,
\mathbf{s}
and
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
satisfy one of the conditions in
Table

1
and

(5.8)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}t^{\prime}_{i}\equiv-1-s^{\prime}_{f-1}+\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}t_{i}\mod p^{f}-1.

Proof.

Existence of such a pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\chi_{1}\qquad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{j}^{e-1}\prod_{i\neq j}\omega_{i}^{s^{\prime}_{i}+e}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t^{\prime}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\qquad

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{s_{i}+e}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}},

\displaystyle\chi_{2}\qquad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{j}^{s^{\prime}_{j}+1}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t^{\prime}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\qquad

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}},

and either
s^{\prime}_{j}\leq p-2
, or
e=1
,
s^{\prime}_{j-1}\neq 0
and
s^{\prime}_{j}=p-1
, for some
j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
.
Translate all indices by a fixed element if necessary so that
j=f-1
. Comparing the two ways of writing
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}
, we obtain the following equivalences mod
p^{f}-1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}(s_{i}+e)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pte-2-s^{\prime}_{f-1}+\sum_{i=0}^{f-2}p^{f-1-i}(s^{\prime}_{i}+e)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}s_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt-2-s^{\prime}_{f-1}+\sum_{i=0}^{f-2}p^{f-1-i}s^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046ptp-s^{\prime}_{f-1}-2+p(s^{\prime}_{f-2}-1)+\sum_{i=0}^{f-3}p^{f-1-i}s^{\prime}_{i}

Therefore, for any fixed
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
, we can find a unique
\mathbf{s}
satisfying the equation above. The uniqueness of
\mathbf{s}
follows from the requirement that each
s_{i}\in[0,p-1]
, and not all
s_{i}
can be
p-1
. Taking all possible (non-Steinberg) values of
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
, we obtain the pairs
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
in
Table

1
.

Comparing the two ways of writing
\chi_{2}
, we obtain mod
p^{f}-1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}t^{\prime}_{i}+(s^{\prime}_{f-1}+1)

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}t_{i}

\displaystyle\iff\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}t^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv-1-s^{\prime}_{f-1}+\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}t_{i}

finishing the proof.
∎

5.2.2.
Case 2:
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
satisfies
Proposition

4.27
3
.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
f>1
,
e>1
. There exists an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and the statement of
Proposition

4.27
3
holding true for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
(in particular,
|J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})|=ef-1
) if and only if, after translating the indices for the components of
\mathbf{t}
,
\mathbf{s}
,
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
by adding a common fixed element of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
if necessary,
\mathbf{s}
and
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
satisfy one of the conditions in
Table

2
and

(5.10)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}t^{\prime}_{i}\equiv-1+\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}t_{i}\mod p^{f}-1.

Proof.

Existence of such a pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\chi_{1}\qquad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{j}^{s^{\prime}_{j}+e-1}\prod_{i\neq j}\omega_{i}^{s^{\prime}_{i}+e}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t^{\prime}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\qquad

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{s_{i}+e}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}},

\displaystyle\chi_{2}\qquad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{j}^{1}\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t^{\prime}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\qquad

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}\omega_{i}^{t_{i}},

and
s^{\prime}_{j}\neq 0
, for some
j\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
.
Translate all indices by a fixed element if necessary so that
j=f-1
.
Writing
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}
in two different ways, we have the following equivalences mod
p^{f}-1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}(s_{i}+e)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle e-2+s^{\prime}_{f-1}+\sum_{i\neq f-1}p^{f-1-i}(s^{\prime}_{i}+e)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}}p^{f-1-i}s_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{f-1}-2+\sum_{i\neq f-1}p^{f-1-i}s^{\prime}_{i}

We plug in all possible (non-Steinberg) values of
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
so that
s^{\prime}_{f-1}\neq 0
and compute the unique (non-Steinberg) value of
\mathbf{s}
so that the above equation is satisfied obtaining the pairs in
Table

2
.

Comparing the two ways of writing
\chi_{2}
, we obtain
Equation

5.10
.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

Let
f>1
and let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be weakly regular. Then there exists a type I intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
if and only if after translating all indices by a fixed element of
\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
and exchanging
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
if necessary, either
Table

1
1
and
Equation

5.8
are true, or
Table

2
2
and
Equation

5.10
are true. Either way, the criteria are not symmetric, and there can exist at most one pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
witnessing a type I intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
.

6.
Type II intersections

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
be non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights. In this section, we will compute criteria for existence of a pair of characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
witnessing a type II intersection between Serre weights
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime\prime}}}
. When such a pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
exists, we will denote by
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
its highest weight. When comparing tuples
\mathbf{s}
,
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}
, we will continue to use the notational conventions we set at the beginning of
Section

5
.

A type II intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
witnessed by
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
implies the existence of type I intersections between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
, and between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
. Therefore, each of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
must satisfy one of the three conditions in
Proposition

4.27
, giving rise to different cases as we will see.

6.1.
Type II intersections when

or

and both satisfy

4.27
3
, then one verifies immediately that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}\cong\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
, a contradiction.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
f=1
. There exists a type II intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
witnessed by an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
if and only if
e>1
,
p>3
and after exchanging
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
if necessary,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
satisfies
Proposition

4.27
1
,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
satisfies
Proposition

4.27
3
,

(6.2)

\displaystyle s^{\prime}=p-3-s,

\displaystyle\qquad s^{\prime\prime}=s+2,

\displaystyle\qquad s^{\prime\prime}=p-1-s^{\prime},\text{ and }

(6.3)

\displaystyle t^{\prime}\equiv t+s+1,

\displaystyle\qquad t^{\prime\prime}\equiv-1+t,

\displaystyle\qquad t^{\prime\prime}\equiv t^{\prime}+s^{\prime}\mod p-1.

Proof.

If there is to be a type II intersection witnessed by
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
, then by
Section

6.1.1
, after exchanging
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
if necessary, we must have
e>1
,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
satisfies
Proposition

4.27
1
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
satisfies
Proposition

4.27
3
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\chi_{1}=\omega^{e-1+t^{\prime}}=\omega^{s^{\prime\prime}+e-1+t^{\prime\prime}}=\omega^{s+e+t},

\displaystyle\chi_{2}=\omega^{s^{\prime}+1+t^{\prime}}=\omega^{1+t^{\prime\prime}}=\omega^{t},

\displaystyle s^{\prime}\leq p-3,

\displaystyle s^{\prime\prime}\neq 0.

Comparing different ways of writing
\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
, we have the following equivalences mod
p-1
:

\displaystyle e-2-s^{\prime}\equiv e-2+s^{\prime\prime}\equiv s+e

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle s^{\prime\prime}\equiv p-1-s^{\prime},\qquad s^{\prime}\equiv p-3-s,\qquad s^{\prime\prime}\equiv s+2.

\displaystyle s^{\prime}+1+t^{\prime}\equiv 1+t^{\prime\prime}\equiv t

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle t^{\prime\prime}\equiv t^{\prime}+s^{\prime},\qquad t^{\prime}\equiv t+s+1,\qquad t^{\prime\prime}\equiv-1+t.

If
s^{\prime}=0
, then we find that
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}\cong\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
, a contradiction. Therefore,
s^{\prime}>0
,
Equations

6.2
and
6.3
are true and
s<p-3
. In particular,
p>3
.

Imposing the above conditions, we next calculate
y^{\prime}
,
y^{\prime\prime}
,
z^{\prime}
,
z^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}
,
\xi^{\prime}
and
\xi^{\prime\prime}
, and compare
J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
with
J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{t^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
.

By the proof of
Proposition

4.27
,
y^{\prime}=s^{\prime}+1
and
z^{\prime}=e-1
, while
y^{\prime\prime}=1
and
z^{\prime\prime}=s^{\prime\prime}+e-1
. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=[0,e-2]

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}=\{1\}\cup[s^{\prime\prime}+1,s^{\prime\prime}+e-2]

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}=(p-1)(e-1)+(e-2-s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime\prime}=(p-1)(s^{\prime\prime}+e-1)+(e-2+s^{\prime\prime})

We have equalities of sets

\{\xi^{\prime}-u^{\prime}(p-1)\>|\>u^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime}\}=\{(p-1)v^{\prime}+(e-2-s^{\prime})\>|\>v^{\prime}\in[1,e-1]\}

and

\displaystyle\{\xi^{\prime\prime}-u^{\prime\prime}(p-1)\>|\>u^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime\prime}\}

\displaystyle=\{(p-1)v^{\prime\prime}+(e-2+s^{\prime\prime})\>|\>v^{\prime\prime}\in[1,e-2]\cup\{s^{\prime\prime}+e-2\}\}

\displaystyle=\{(p-1)v^{\prime\prime}+(e-2-s^{\prime})+(p-1)\>|\>v^{\prime\prime}\in[1,e-2]\cup\{p-3-s^{\prime}+e\}\}

\displaystyle=\{(p-1)v^{\prime\prime}+(e-2-s^{\prime})\>|\>v^{\prime\prime}\in[2,e-1]\cup\{p-2-s^{\prime}+e\}\}.

Hence,
J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})=J^{\mathrm{AH}}_{\sigma_{t^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
if and only if for all
v^{\prime}\in[1,e-1]
, there exists a
v^{\prime\prime}\in[2,e-1]\cup\{p-2-s^{\prime}+e\}
such that:

(6.4)

\displaystyle\frac{(p-1)v^{\prime}+(e-2-s^{\prime})}{p^{\nu^{\prime}}}=\frac{(p-1)v^{\prime\prime}+(e-2-s^{\prime})}{p^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}}

where
\nu^{\prime}
is the
p
-adic valuation of the numerator on L.H.S, while
\nu^{\prime\prime}
is that of the numerator on R.H.S.

The only thing to check then is that
Equation

6.4
holds for
v^{\prime}=1
and
v^{\prime\prime}=p-2-s^{\prime}+e
. Plugging in,

\displaystyle\mathrm{R.H.S.}

\displaystyle=\frac{(p-1)(p-2-s^{\prime}+e)+e-2-s^{\prime}}{p^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p(p-3-s^{\prime}+e)}{p^{\nu^{\prime}+1}}=\mathrm{L.H.S.}

finishing the proof.
∎

Remark 6.5
.

If
s^{\prime\prime}\leq p-3
, the relationship between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
is symmetric in
Equations

6.2
and
6.3
but if
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
are exchanged,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
changes. Therefore, if the criteria hold, there exist two distinct ordered pairs of
I_{K}
-characters witnessing a type II intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
.

On the other hand, if
s^{\prime\prime}=p-2
and
s^{\prime}=1
, then the roles of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
cannot be exchanged since
p>3
. Therefore, if
Equations

6.2
and
6.3
hold in this setting, then there exists exactly one pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
witnessing a type II intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
.

We note also that
Equations

6.2
and
6.3
are true for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
(for suitable
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
) if and only if they satisfy the conditions of
Proposition

2.4
1
1(b)
.

Remark 6.8
.

Note that in each pair of
\mathbf{s^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{s^{\prime\prime}}
featuring in
Table

3
, at least one is not weakly regular. Therefore, when
f>1
and
e=1
, every type II intersection involves at least one Serre weight that is not weakly regular.

6.3.
Type II intersections when
f>1
,
e>1

We set up some extra notation that we will need in the following calculations. Let
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
be an ordered pair of
I_{K}
-characters so that if
\tilde{\chi}_{1}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{2}
are extensions of
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
respectively to
G_{K}
, then
L^{\star}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
and
L^{\star}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}}(\tilde{\chi}_{1},\tilde{\chi}_{2})
are non-empty. In particular, for each
i\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}
,
\xi^{\prime}_{i}\equiv\lambda^{\prime}_{i}\equiv\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{i}\equiv\xi^{\prime\prime}_{i}\mod f
. Therefore, for each
i
, we can define
V^{\prime}_{i},V^{\prime\prime}_{i}\subset\mathbf{Z}
so that

\displaystyle\{\xi^{\prime}_{i}-u(p^{f}-1)\;|\;u\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=\{(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}\;|\;v\in V^{\prime}_{i}\}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\{\xi^{\prime\prime}_{i}-u(p^{f}-1)\;|\;u\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\}

\displaystyle=\{(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}\;|\;v\in V^{\prime\prime}_{i}\}

and further define
P^{\prime},P^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}
as follows:

\displaystyle P^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\{(i,v)\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}\;|\;v\in V^{\prime}_{i}\}\>\text{ and}

\displaystyle P^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=\{(i,v)\in\mathbf{Z}/f\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}\;|\;v\in V^{\prime\prime}_{i}\}.

Define a function

\beta\colon P^{\prime}\cup P^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbf{Z}\times[0,f^{\prime\prime}-1]

by setting

\displaystyle\beta(i,v)=\begin{cases}\alpha^{\prime}(i,u)&\text{ if }(i,v)\in P^{\prime},\\
\alpha^{\prime\prime}(i,u)&\text{ if }(i,v)\in P^{\prime\prime}\end{cases}

where
u
satisfies

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}_{i}-u(p^{f}-1)=(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}\>

\displaystyle\text{ if }(i,v)\in P^{\prime},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime\prime}_{i}-u(p^{f}-1)=(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}\>

\displaystyle\text{ if }(i,v)\in P^{\prime\prime}.

Note that there is no ambiguity in the definition of
\beta(i,v)
when
(i,v)\in P^{\prime}\cap P^{\prime\prime}
. Furthermore,
\beta|_{P^{\prime}}
and
\beta|_{P^{\prime\prime}}
are injective functions and
J^{\text{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})=\beta(P^{\prime})
while
J^{\text{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})=\beta(P^{\prime\prime})
.

Lemma 6.9
.

J^{\text{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})=J^{\text{AH}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
if and only if
\beta(P^{\prime}\smallsetminus P^{\prime\prime})=\beta(P^{\prime\prime}\smallsetminus P^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Clear.
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

Fix
(i,v)\in P^{\prime}
. There exists
(j,w)\in P^{\prime\prime}
such that
\beta(i,v)=\beta(j,w)
if and only if the following are true:

(1)

(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}=p^{\nu}((p^{f}-1)w+\lambda^{\prime}_{j})
for some
\nu\in\mathbf{Z}
, and

(2)

i-j\equiv\nu\mod f
.

Proof.

Let
\beta(i,v)=(n_{1},\kappa_{1})
and
\beta(j,w)=(n_{2},\kappa_{2})
, and let
\nu_{1}
be the
p
-adic valuation of
(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}
and
\nu_{2}=\nu_{1}+\nu
be the
p
-adic valuation of
(p^{f}-1)w+\lambda^{\prime}_{j}
. Further, let
i_{n_{1}}
and
i_{n_{2}}
be the unique representatives in
[0,f^{\prime}-1]
of
i-\nu_{1}
and
j-\nu_{2}
respectively. From the definition of
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
, we see that the equality
n_{1}=n_{2}
holds if and only if
(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}=p^{\nu}((p^{f}-1)w+\lambda^{\prime}_{j})
. This in turn implies
i_{n_{1}}=i_{n_{2}}
and

\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}\equiv\frac{(i-j)-\nu}{f^{\prime}}\mod f^{\prime\prime}.

Therefore, if
n_{1}=n_{2}
holds, then
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}
if and only if
i-j\equiv\nu\mod f.

∎

Corollary 6.11
.

Let
(i,v)\in P^{\prime}
and
(j,w)\in P^{\prime\prime}
satisfy one of the following:

(1)

j=i+1
and
w=pv+z^{\prime}_{i+1}-y^{\prime}_{i+1}
, or

(2)

j=i-1
and
v=pw+z^{\prime}_{i}-y^{\prime}_{i}
.

Then
\beta(i,v)=\beta(j,w)
.

Proof.

Plugging into the expression for
\lambda^{\prime}_{i}
and
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}
, in the first situation we get

(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}=\frac{(p^{f}-1)w+\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}{p}

and in the second, we get

(p^{f}-1)v+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}=p((p^{f}-1)w+\lambda^{\prime}_{j}).

An application of
Lemma

6.10
finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 6.14
.

For each pair (
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
) as in the statement of

Proof.

Using
Lemma

6.9
, we need to show that for
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
as in each row of

\beta(P^{\prime}\smallsetminus P^{\prime\prime})\neq\beta(P^{\prime\prime}\smallsetminus P^{\prime})

We have

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }i\neq f-1,\\
s^{\prime}_{i}+1&\text{if }i=f-1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\qquad z^{\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}s^{\prime}_{i}+e&\text{if }i\neq f-1\\
e-1&\text{if }i=f-1\end{cases}

\displaystyle y^{\prime\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }i\neq l,\\
s^{\prime\prime}_{i}+1&\text{if }i=l\end{cases}

\displaystyle\qquad z^{\prime\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}s^{\prime\prime}_{i}+e&\text{if }i\neq l\\
e-1&\text{if }i=l\end{cases}

and further,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle V^{\prime\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}[1,e-1]\cup\{z^{\prime}_{i}-y^{\prime}_{i}\}&\qquad\text{if }i\not\in\{l\}\cup[r,f-1]\\

\end{cases}

6.11
, we only need to show that

. Assume otherwise. The
p

. Equivalently, by

. Hence,

\displaystyle(p^{f}-1)e+\lambda^{\prime}_{r}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(p^{f}-1)(z^{\prime}_{f-1}-y^{\prime}_{f-1})+\lambda^{\prime}_{f-1}}{p^{f-1-r}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{j=-1}^{f-2}p^{f-1-j}(z^{\prime}_{j}-y^{\prime}_{j})}{p^{f-1-r}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=-1}^{r}p^{r-j}(z^{\prime}_{j}-y^{\prime}_{j})+\sum_{j=r+1}^{f-2}\frac{z^{\prime}_{j}-y^{\prime}_{j}}{p^{j-r}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{r+1}p^{j}(z^{\prime}_{r-j}-y^{\prime}_{r-j})+p^{-f}\sum_{j=r+2}^{f-1}p^{j}(z^{\prime}_{r-j}-y^{\prime}_{r-j})

\displaystyle<(p^{f}-1)e+\lambda^{\prime}_{r}

giving rise to a contradiction.

,
5
and
5

\displaystyle V^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}[1,e-1]\cup\{z^{\prime}_{i}-y^{\prime}_{i}\}&\qquad\qquad\text{if }i\not=f-1\\

Proposition 6.20
.

For each pair (

,

if and only if after translating indices by adding a fixed element of

Proof.

Using

, we need to show that for

We have

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }i\neq f-1,\\
s^{\prime}_{i}+1&\text{if }i=f-1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\qquad z^{\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}s^{\prime}_{i}+e&\qquad\text{if }i\neq f-1\\

\displaystyle y^{\prime\prime}_{i}=\begin{cases}0&\qquad\text{if }i\neq l,\\

and further,

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\{0\}\cup[s^{\prime}_{i}+1,s^{\prime}_{i}+e-1]&\text{if }i\neq f-1\\
[0,e-2]&\text{if }i=f-1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\{0\}\cup[s^{\prime\prime}_{i}+1,s^{\prime\prime}_{i}+e-1]&\text{if }i\neq l\\
\{1\}\cup[s^{\prime\prime}_{i}+1,s^{\prime\prime}_{i}+e-2]&\text{if }i=l.\end{cases}

\displaystyle V^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}[1,e-1]\cup\{z^{\prime}_{i}-y^{\prime}_{i}\}&\text{if }i\neq f-1\\

.
