Remark 6.21
.

Let
e>1
,
f>1
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
be a pair of weakly regular Serre weights. An examination of
Tables

5
and
6
shows that there exists a type II intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
if and only if
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime\prime}}})\neq 0
via the criterion described in
Proposition

2.4
2
. Moreover, the relationship between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime\prime}}
is asymmetric, and therefore, there exists at most one pair of
I_{K}
-characters witnessing a type II intersection between them.

7.
Conclusion

From the findings of
Sections

5
and
6
, we get our main results:

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be a pair of non-isomorphic, non-Steinberg Serre weights. Then

(7.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}})\neq 0\implies\dim{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}=[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1.

If
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
are further assumed to be weakly regular, then
Equation

7.2
upgrades to

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}})\neq 0\iff\dim{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}=[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1.

Proof.

Consider the explicit numerical criterion for type I and II intersections in
Propositions

5.1
,
5.4
and
6.1
when
f=1
, in
Propositions

5.7
and
6.6
when
f>1
and
e=1
, and in
Propositions

5.7
,
5.9
,
6.14
,
6.17
and
6.20
when
f>1
and
e>1
.

The result follows from considering these numerical criteria in conjunction with
Theorem

4.38
as well as the criteria for

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})
to be non-zero given in
Propositions

2.4
and
2.14
and
Theorems

2.20
and
2.26
.

We note that a complete (and non-empty) list of pairs of Serre weights
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
satisfying
\dim{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}=[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
and
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})=0
(and so, necessarily, one of
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
is not weakly regular and
f>1
) is obtained by combining data from
Tables

1
and
3
when
e=1
, and
Tables

1
and
2
along with the rows marked with an asterisk in
Tables

5
and
6
when
e>1
.
∎

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
be a pair of weakly regular and non-isomorphic Serre weights, so that
\dim\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}=[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
. Then the following are true:

(1)

Let
n
be the number of top-dimensional irreducible components in
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}
, that is, irreducible components of dimension
[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]-1
. If
e=1
, then
n=1
. If
e>1
, then

\displaystyle n=\begin{cases}2&\text{ if }\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})\neq 0,\\
1&\text{ if }\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}[\GL_{2}(k)]}(\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}},\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}})=0.\end{cases}

(2)

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be a top-dimensional irreducible component in
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{s^{\prime}}}}
. If
e=1
, then
{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma}
for a non-Steinberg Serre weight
\sigma
implies that
\sigma
is isomorphic to either
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
or
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
. If either
e>1
,
f=1
and
s,s^{\prime}<p-3
, or
e>1
and
f>1
, then there exists a non-Steinberg
\sigma
not isomorphic to either
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
or
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
so that
{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from
Remark

5.3
when
f=1
and
e=1
,
Remarks

5.3
,
5.6
and
6.5
when
f=1
and
e>1
, and
Remarks

5.12
,
6.8
and
6.21
when
f>1
, by comparing with
Theorem

4.38
and
Proposition

2.4
.

For the second statement, suppose first that
K\neq\mathbf{Q}_{p}
. The proof of
Theorem

3.16
shows that there exist
G_{K}
-characters

\tilde{\chi}_{1}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{2}
lifting
I_{K}
-characters
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
respectively so that

{\mathcal{Z}}
is in the closure of a family
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1},\tilde{\chi}_{1}^{-1}}
contained in
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}}\cap{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}}|
. Using the proof of
Theorem

4.38
, the existence of such a top-dimensional component
{\mathcal{Z}}
and such
G_{K}
-characters
\tilde{\chi}_{1}
and
\tilde{\chi_{2}}
is equivalent to
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
having either a type I or a type II intersection witnessed by
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
.

If it is a type II intersection, let
\sigma
be the highest weight (see
Definition

4.26
) for the pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
. Then
\sigma
is non-Steinberg and not isomorphic to either
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
or
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
, and moreover,
{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma}
as described in the proof of
Proposition

4.45
. When
e=1
,
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
do not have a type II intersection by
Remark

6.8
. This deals with the case of type II intersections between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
, and we may now assume that the pair
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
witnesses a type I intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
, with highest weight
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
. The existence of a non-Steinberg
\sigma
not isomorphic to either
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
or
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and such that
{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma}
is equivalent to
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
witnessing a type II intersection between
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
and
\sigma
. The desired statement then follows from comparing
Propositions

5.1
and
5.4
with
Proposition

6.1
when
f=1
,
Table

1
1
with
Table

3
when
f>1
and
e=1
, and
Table

1
1
and
Table

2
2
with
Table

6
6
when
f>1
and
e>1
.

Next, suppose
K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
. If
{\mathcal{Z}}
is in the closure of a family
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1},\tilde{\chi}_{1}^{-1}}
as above, then the argument above goes through unchanged. Otherwise, by the proof of
Theorem

3.16
, there exists an irreducible two-dimensional
G_{K}
-representation
\overline{\rho}
so that
{\mathcal{Z}}
is in the scheme-theoretic image of the map
f_{\overline{\rho}}
defined in
Equation

3.13
. By
Lemma

4.35
and
Proposition

4.36
, the scheme-theoretic image of
f_{\overline{\rho}}
can be contained in at most two irreducible components of
{\mathcal{X}}
and therefore, there does not exist
\sigma
non-Steinberg and not isomorphic to either
\sigma_{\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s}}
or
\sigma_{\mathbf{t}^{\prime},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
such that
{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma}
. This finishes the proof. ∎
